# Lab Tracker — Deep Codebase Review

Date: 2026-09-21 · Reviewed commit: `a025e76` (main, 2026-08-15) · Previous full review: [deep-review-2026-06-12.md](deep-review-2026-06-12.md)

**Method.** 35 parallel area reviewers (27 subsystem areas, 6 cross-cutting sweeps, one regression check of the June review's HIGH findings), each reading its files in full and reproducing claims with scratch tests where possible. Every medium-or-higher finding was then re-examined by an independent verifier (reproduce, check prior art and intent, assess reachability per deployment mode, calibrate severity); every high or critical, and every uncertain verdict, additionally went to an adversarial refuter instructed to disprove it. Low findings received one calibration pass with refute-by-default. Duplicates were folded across areas. The lead session independently reproduced the critical finding and hand-checked fourteen of the highs. In total 129 agent runs completed (35 finders, 93 verifiers, 1 completeness critic); about 200 further launches were lost to three usage-limit interruptions and re-run from cached state. Severities follow one scale: critical = auth bypass, cross-project exposure or data loss in a supported configuration; high = security or data-integrity bug reachable in supported use, or a documented workflow that does not work; medium = correctness bug in realistic use or a significant operational risk; low = hygiene or documentation.

**Stats.** 295 raw findings → 259 after folding 40 duplicates → **225 confirmed** (1 critical, 10 high, 83 medium, 131 low), 32 refuted, 2 disputed or unresolved. All 12 HIGH findings from the June review are fixed.

## Executive summary

Lab Tracker at this commit is a well-layered, security-conscious codebase with unusually strong engineering discipline for a research tool. Routes only resolve the actor and delegate; authorization lives in one policy class and is enforced with an opaque-404 contract that a certified inventory test pins; the outbound HTTP, store-authority and bounded-subprocess layers are among the most careful I have seen in a project of this size; and the test suite (4,924 cases, 89% line coverage, hand-written fakes instead of mocks, real Postgres race tests) catches most regressions before they ship. Every high-severity problem from the June review has been fixed, and the 200 commits since then added large, well-tested subsystems (store authority, artifact resolution, hosted MCP, member onboarding).

The review still found one critical defect and ten high ones, and they share a shape: the code that runs inside the request path is strong, while the seams around it are not. The critical finding (C1) is a data-loss bug in SQLite, which is the default backend and a supported production backend for single-user and LAN deployments. Since July, Alembic's engine runs with `PRAGMA foreign_keys=ON`, and several migrations rebuild parent tables in SQLite batch mode; the rebuild's `DROP TABLE` fires `ON DELETE CASCADE` and `SET NULL` on every child. Any SQLite database stamped at revision 0056 through 0060 (instances last migrated between 25 July and 13 August 2026) silently loses every `graph_change_operations` row and all `change_set_id` backlinks on `alembic upgrade head`, which the server runs automatically at startup. I reproduced this against the real migration chain. It needs a fix, a regression test that starts from seeded databases at each affected stamp, and an operator advisory.

The highs fall into four groups. First, the release is not installable or runnable as documented: the widened `mcp<3` range breaks the MCP server on any non-lockfile install (H2), the root compose file refuses to start the app at all until two MCP tokens exist that can only be minted from a running app (H1), an import-time `SessionLocal` drags full settings validation into every `lab_tracker` import and breaks `lt`, `lt-mcp` and the deployment probe when settings are incomplete (H5), and the Linux daily-review installer writes a crontab that Debian's cron rejects (H3). Second, credential lifecycle and proxy trust: personal access tokens keep the role they were minted with after a demotion and cannot be revoked by an admin (H4); the first-admin bootstrap token is disclosed to any private-address peer, which behind the documented reverse proxy or Docker bridge is everyone (H8); and the client leaks embedded remote-URL credentials into shared notes (H10, H9). Third, the hosted MCP surface is not bounded: entity ids are spliced into URL paths unescaped and httpx collapses `..`, so a tool argument can retarget any route the token may call (H7); combined with the hosted server's fail-open admin guard (M36) that is any route the held token's scope allows. Fourth, offline capture never drains in auth-disabled mode because the queue requires a bearer token that mode never issues (H6).

The medium findings cluster into recognisable families rather than isolated slips: referential integrity on delete is enforced per entity in ad-hoc lists, so exploration nodes, manifest note ids, note targets, superseded links and claim junctions dangle or cascade silently (M55, M51, M67, M63, M68, M76); ORM metadata and the migrated schema have drifted with no parity test (M23, M81) and SQLite migrations are neither atomic nor consistently FK-safe (M14, M15); the MCP hosted server registers write and local-file tools, fails open on its admin guard and rejects proxied Hosts (M35, M36, M34); the in-memory rate limiter grows without bound on attacker-chosen keys (M43) and session JWTs cannot be revoked (M44); several frontend loaders lack stale-response guards or in-flight locks (M26, M28, M27); and the container runs uvicorn as PID 1 without an init, so a timed-out subprocess reports a cleanup failure and leaks a zombie (M22).

## Quality gates (run on this clone)

| Gate | Result |
| --- | --- |
| `uv run ruff check .` | pass |
| `uv run mypy` | pass, 68 files |
| `uv run pytest -q` | **2 failed**, 4794 passed, 128 skipped, 13m15s; coverage 89% |
| `alembic heads` | one head, `0062_member_onboarding_purpose` |
| `npm run lint:frontend` / `typecheck:frontend` / `check:frontend-contracts` | pass |
| `npm run test:frontend` | 341 passed in 43 files |
| `npm run build:frontend` + bundle drift | clean, bundle byte-identical |
| skill reference `--check` | current |
| `npm run test:e2e` | 10 passed once pointed at the preinstalled Chromium; the stock config fails only because this container's browser revision differs from the locked Playwright |
| `uv lock --check` | **fails** at HEAD (finding M83) |
| `npm audit --audit-level=high` | **1 high** (browserslist, via eslint-plugin-react-hooks), so the CI audit gate is red on main |

The two pytest failures are both in `tests/test_bounded_subprocess.py` and are environmental in origin: this sandbox's PID 1 reaps orphaned zombies only every ~1.9 s, longer than the 1.25 s cleanup bound. The same mechanism is a real defect in the shipped container, where uvicorn is PID 1 and never reaps at all (M22). The 128 skips are the Postgres-marked and platform-specific tests; three Postgres race tests are skipped in CI too because they lack the marker (M82).

## What to fix first

1. **C1 SQLite migration data loss.** In `alembic/env.py` turn the FK pragma off on the raw connection before `run_migrations()` and run `PRAGMA foreign_key_check` afterwards, failing loudly; remove the per-migration toggles (M15); add a regression test that seeds a database at 0060, 0029 and 0034 and asserts child rows survive `upgrade head`; publish an advisory for SQLite operators who upgraded between 25 July and 13 August.
2. **Make the release installable again.** Pin `mcp<2` or migrate to the 2.x API, regenerate `uv.lock`, add `uv lock --check` to CI and move dependabot to the `uv` ecosystem (H2, M83, M2); put the compose `mcp` service behind a profile or make its tokens optional and fix `.env.example` (H1, M1, M4); replace `uv run` in the container command with `python -m` (M5); delete the dead `SessionLocal` (H5); add the trailing newline to the crontab pipe (H3); fix the docs' non-Docker first-admin steps (M6).
3. **Credential lifecycle and proxy trust.** Re-read the live role for PAT principals and add admin listing and revocation (H4); add session revocation or an absolute lifetime (M44); default bootstrap disclosure to `never` outside `local` and refuse `local` mode without a trusted-proxy configuration, and ship `--proxy-headers --forwarded-allow-ips` in the image for the documented topology (H8); bound and evict rate-limit buckets (M43).
4. **Bound the MCP surface.** Validate ids as UUIDs and percent-encode path segments in the API client (H7); register only read tools on the hosted server and verify the held token is read-only (M35); make the admin guard fail closed (M36); make Host validation configurable (M34).
5. **Client credential hygiene.** One shared remote-URL normaliser that strips any userinfo, used by repo, git snapshot and figure capture (H10, H9); raise the 1 s git timeout and fail loud instead of recording `git_dirty: False` (M79).
6. **Offline capture and frontend races.** Drain the queue on owner identity alone when auth is disabled (H6); add in-flight and stale-response guards to capture, the schedule form and the batches page (M28, M26, L57); stop the note PATCH from replaying stale metadata (M27).
7. **Deletion and ownership integrity.** Replace the per-entity guard lists with one reference registry consulted by every delete and by ownership reassignment (M55, M51, M63, M68, M67, M76); lock the dataset-delete and claim-edge cycle checks on Postgres (M56, M52); close the group-detach, last-group-owner and offboarding-export gaps (M64, M65, M66).
8. **Schema and test parity.** Add an ORM-vs-Alembic parity test and reconcile the 32 drift items (M23, M81); mark the three Postgres race tests (M82); add `init: true` to the compose services (M22); make SQLite migrations atomic or at least clean up `_alembic_tmp` (M14).

## What is strong

- **Authorization model.** One `ProjectAuthorizationPolicy`, opaque 404s on every detail read, list scoping through `accessible_project_ids`, same-project checks on every cross-entity link, and a read-opacity inventory certified against the OpenAPI operation ids. The authorization sweep inventoried about 190 endpoints and 43 MCP tools and found no cross-project read or IDOR.
- **Outbound network and host I/O boundary.** DNS-pinned connections with full special-range rejection, per-hop redirect re-authorisation, one monotonic deadline across DNS, TLS, headers and body; typed store-authority proofs required by every remote target constructor; handle-bound, output-free local traversal in isolated helpers; structurally parsed Git and rclone remotes with no shell.
- **Transactions and idempotency.** One commit decision per command with after-commit hooks, savepoint-isolated idempotent creates backed by real unique constraints, transactional outboxes with `SKIP LOCKED` on Postgres and conditional updates on SQLite, blob writes ordered after row locks with cleanup on rollback.
- **Test discipline.** Engine and session lifecycle enforced with `ResourceWarning` promoted to error, `FakeClock` instead of sleeps, real Postgres race tests with `pg_blocking_pids`, and drift gates for the bundle, the OpenAPI types and the skill reference.
- **Documentation posture.** The retained-surface document is treated as authoritative, configuration docs are parity-tested against `Settings`, and long-form design documents are dated and status-labelled.

## Limitations of this review

- No Postgres server and no Docker daemon were available. Postgres-specific behaviour (advisory locks, `SKIP LOCKED`, migration fencing) and container builds were assessed by reading and by replaying the relevant commands in scratch directories, not executed end to end. Windows-only code paths were read, not run.
- The June review's 71 MEDIUM findings were not re-checked; only its 12 HIGH findings were (all fixed). A follow-up should sweep the mediums, since several themes above (deletion guards, rate limiter, proxy trust) were already open then.
- Verification ran as one verifier plus an adversarial refuter on every high and every uncertain verdict, rather than the two-lens-plus-refuter design originally planned; low findings had a single pass. Verifier reasoning is summarised under each finding.
- Each area reviewer lists the files it did not read completely under "Not fully read" in its assessment. The largest declared gaps are the 6,800-line artifact-resolution test module, the Windows helper tests, and the bodies of the Postgres race tests.
