## Confirmed findings

### CRITICAL (1)

#### C1. SQLite batch-mode table rebuilds cascade-delete child rows (0061 wipes graph_change_operations and nulls change_set_id backlinks)

`src/lab_tracker/alembic/versions/0061_graph_draft_generation_fencing.py:19` · data-integrity · area: db-models-migrations

Since commit 1fc76cc (2026-07-26) env.py calls configure_sqlite_engine(connectable) on Alembic's own engine, so every migration connection starts with PRAGMA foreign_keys=ON. Alembic's SQLite batch mode implements any op that needs a rebuild (drop_column, alter_column, create_foreign_key/unique/check constraint, add_column with a ClauseElement server_default) as CREATE _alembic_tmp -> INSERT ... SELECT -> DROP TABLE <old> -> RENAME. With foreign keys enabled, SQLite's DROP TABLE performs an implicit DELETE FROM that fires ON DELETE CASCADE / SET NULL actions on every referencing table. Several migrations rebuild parent tables without disabling the pragma: 0061 (graph_change_sets: create_check_constraint + create_index -> rebuild; children graph_change_operations CASCADE and questions/datasets/notes/sessions/analyses/claims/goals/visualizations/experiments/entity_versions/batch_runs/outbox .change_set_id SET NULL), 0030 (goals -> goal_links), 0035 (claims, visualizations and all origin tables -> claim_*, visualization_claims, note_targets, graph_change_sets, acquisition_outputs, …

**Evidence.** Scratch reproduction (scratchpad/q1/cascade2.py, cascade3.py) against the real chain: === A_0060_to_0061: graph_change_operations: before=1 after=0 <<< CHANGED; questions with change_set_id: before=1 after=0 <<< CHANGED === B_0060_to_head: same loss === B2_0056_to_head: same loss (0057..0060 contain no pragma toggle, so FK stays ON until 0061) === C_0029_to_0030: goal_links: before=1 after=0 === D_0007_to_0008: questions: before=1 after=0; notes: before=1 after=0 === E_0034_to_0035_no_datasets: question_parents, claim_analyses, claim_questions, visualizations, visualization_claims, note_targets, graph_change_sets: all before=1 after=0 === G_0034_to_0035_with_dataset: MIGRATION FAILED …

**Failure scenario.** A single-user or LAN lab server on SQLite (the default DB) was last started at revision 0057-0060 (July/August 2026). Operator pulls current main and starts the app; startup runs `alembic upgrade head`; 0061 rebuilds graph_change_sets with FK enforcement on; every graph_change_operations row (all AI draft proposals with their acceptance_mode/accepted_by provenance) is deleted and every committed entity's …

**Suggested fix.** Make batch rebuilds unconditionally safe: in env.py, for SQLite execute `PRAGMA foreign_keys=OFF` on the raw connection before `context.run_migrations()` (outside any transaction) and run `PRAGMA foreign_key_check` after each migration, failing loudly on violations; alternatively add a shared helper used by every migration that performs the toggle via `op.get_context().autocommit_block()`. Remove the per-migration ad-hoc toggles or make them use …

**Verification.** verify=confirmed(critical), refute=confirmed(critical)


### HIGH (10)

#### H1. Root docker-compose `${VAR:?}` for MCP tokens makes `docker compose up app` and every documented backup/restore command fail unless MCP tokens are set

`docker-compose.yml:71` · correctness · area: deploy-packaging

Compose interpolates the whole file before selecting services, so the `:?` requirements on `LT_MCP_INBOUND_TOKEN` / `LT_MCP_READONLY_TOKEN` in the `mcp` service abort every compose invocation, including `docker compose up app` (docs/deployment-options.md:41, docs/lab-tracker-mcp-skills.md:216), `docker compose up postgres` (docs/lan-shared-graph.md:22), and all `docker compose exec/stop/up -d app` commands in docs/self-hosted-operations.md (lines 44-99). Labs that do not use the optional MCP service cannot run the primary Docker/Postgres path as documented. The shared-provider compose test only passes because it exports both tokens itself.

**Evidence.** `env -u LT_MCP_INBOUND_TOKEN -u LT_MCP_READONLY_TOKEN docker compose -f docker-compose.yml config --services app` -> `error while interpolating services.mcp.environment.LAB_TRACKER_MCP_INBOUND_TOKEN: required variable LT_MCP_INBOUND_TOKEN is missing a value: Set LT_MCP_INBOUND_TOKEN to a distinct random bearer token`. With both tokens set the same command lists postgres/app/mcp. tests/test_shared_provider_deployment.py:53-54 sets `LT_MCP_READONLY_TOKEN`/`LT_MCP_INBOUND_TOKEN` to placeholders to make `docker compose config` succeed.

**Failure scenario.** Operator follows docs/deployment-options.md 'Docker/Postgres Lab Instance': `docker compose up app` exits immediately with the interpolation error; the same happens for the backup command in docs/self-hosted-operations.md, so an operator trying to back up before an upgrade is blocked.

**Suggested fix.** Move the `mcp` service behind a compose profile (e.g. `profiles: [mcp]`) or drop the `:?` form in favour of a fail-fast check inside the MCP process (mcp_server.py already raises when LAB_TRACKER_MCP_INBOUND_TOKEN is missing), and update docs to say `docker compose --profile mcp up mcp`.

**Verification.** verify=confirmed(high), refute=confirmed(high), lead=confirmed(high)

#### H2. Documented fresh-clone install resolves mcp 2.x and breaks `lt-mcp` (and the test suite) at import

`pyproject.toml:34` · packaging · area: docs-drift · also reported as mcp-1, deploy-packaging-1

The most recent commit (a025e76, dependabot #195) widened the dependency to `mcp>=1.27,<3`, but `src/lab_tracker/mcp_server.py:13` still does `from mcp.server.fastmcp import FastMCP`, and `tests/test_mcp_server.py:14` imports `mcp.server.fastmcp.exceptions`. Every documented install path that does not use the lockfile now resolves mcp 2.2.0 and produces a non-functional MCP server: CLAUDE.md:67 and docs/setup.md:32 (`uv pip install -e ".[test,lint]"`), README.md:192 (`uv pip install -e .`), docs/windows-fresh-clone.md:33 (`pip install -e`), the Setup page's `uv tool install --force "<pinned requirement>"` / `uv add` commands (src/lab_tracker/frontend_src/features/client-setup.js:22-24) that every consumer machine runs, and the `uvx --from git+https://github.com/SamuelBrudner/lab-tracker lt-mcp` fallback in docs/lab-tracker-copilot.md:100 and docs/lab-tracker-cursor.md:82. CI and the Dockerfile use `uv sync --frozen` (uv.lock pins mcp 1.27.2), so the breakage is invisible to CI while being the default for humans and consumer installs. `uv run pytest -q` on a fresh clone installed per …

**Evidence.** Fresh venv, `uv pip install --dry-run -e "/home/user/lab-tracker[test,lint]"` -> `+ mcp==2.2.0`. Installing the project from `git archive HEAD` into that venv and running `lt-mcp`: ``` from mcp.server.fastmcp import FastMCP ModuleNotFoundError: No module named 'mcp.server.fastmcp'. This is mcp 2.x, where FastMCP was renamed to MCPServer ... or pin 'mcp<2' to keep running v1 code. ``` `git log -1`: `a025e76 build(deps): Update mcp requirement from <2,>=1.27 to >=1.27,<3 (#195)`; `uv.lock` still has `name = "mcp" version = "1.27.2"`; `.github/workflows/ci.yml:30` uses `uv sync --frozen`.

**Failure scenario.** A researcher follows README/setup.md (or the Setup page's `uv tool install` command) on a new machine, configures `.mcp.json` per docs, and Claude Code/Cursor report the lab-tracker MCP server failed to start (ModuleNotFoundError). `lt setup verify-mcp` fails; `uv run pytest` on a fresh clone errors during collection of tests/test_mcp_server.py, test_mcp_evidence_bundle.py, test_auth_cli.py.

**Suggested fix.** Either revert to `mcp>=1.27,<2` until the server is ported to the 2.x `MCPServer` API, or port `mcp_server.py`/`mcp_tools` and tests to mcp 2.x. Add a CI job that installs from `pyproject.toml` without `--frozen` (or a `uv lock --check` plus an unlocked smoke install) and runs `lt-mcp --help`/imports, so a dependency-range bump cannot silently break the published install commands.

**Verification.** verify=confirmed(high), refute=confirmed(high), lead=confirmed(high)

#### H3. Cron installer pipes a crontab without a trailing newline; Debian/Ubuntu crontab refuses to install it

`scripts/install-daily-review.sh:85` · correctness · area: review-email-scheduling

`MERGED="$(... merge-crontab ...)"` strips the trailing newline that merge_crontab() appends, and the installer then feeds it to cron with `printf '%s' "$MERGED" | crontab -`, i.e. with no newline before EOF. Debian's cron (the `cron` package used by Debian and Ubuntu, 3.0pl1-*) checks for this in crontab.c replace_cmd() and aborts with "new crontab file is missing newline before EOF, can't install." The installer therefore exits non-zero on the primary Linux target after already having written the secrets file, and no cron entry is installed. The unit tests do not catch this because TestCronInstallerAdapter stubs `crontab` with `cat > file`. cronie (Fedora/RHEL) and macOS cron do not enforce the check, so the bug is platform-specific to the very distribution the doc's "Linux" instructions target.

**Evidence.** scripts/install-daily-review.sh:63-65 `MERGED="$("$PYTHON" "$SCHED" merge-crontab ...)"` (command substitution strips trailing newlines); line 85 `printf '%s' "$MERGED" | crontab -`. scripts/scheduler_install.py:358 returns `"\n".join(kept) + "\n"` but that newline is lost. Debian cron source (sources.debian.org, cron 3.0pl1-197, crontab.c replace_cmd): `if (nl == FALSE) { fprintf(stderr, "new crontab file is missing newline before " "EOF, can't install.\n");`. tests/test_scheduler_install.py:335-339 stub: `cat > "{installed}"` so the suite never exercises a real crontab.

**Failure scenario.** On Ubuntu/Debian, a user runs `scripts/install-daily-review.sh` as documented in docs/scheduled-daily-review.md. crontab prints "new crontab file is missing newline before EOF, can't install.", the script exits 1 (set -e), ~/.config/lab-tracker/daily-review.secrets.json has been written, and no scheduled job exists; the daily review never fires.

**Suggested fix.** Use `printf '%s\n' "$MERGED" | crontab -` (or write merge output to a temp file and `crontab "$file"`), and add a test whose stub crontab rejects input lacking a trailing newline.

**Verification.** verify=confirmed(high), refute=confirmed(high), lead=confirmed(medium)

#### H4. Personal access tokens keep their issuance-time role after the user is demoted; admins cannot revoke other users' tokens

`src/lab_tracker/app_parts/middleware.py:216` · authz · area: auth

For lpat_ principals the middleware builds the AuthContext from `principal.role`, which `PersonalAccessTokenService.verify_token` reads straight from the stored row (`role=Role(row.role)`, src/lab_tracker/auth.py:1238). The role is capped only at issuance (`_cap_role`, auth.py:1168). Session JWTs and device tokens, by contrast, use the live `user.role` (middleware lines 183 and 230). So when an admin demotes a user (PATCH /auth/users/{id}), every PAT that user minted while admin keeps admin authority (global project read via `ProjectAuthorizationPolicy.has_global_read`, plus writes/deletes if `read_only=false`) for up to `PERSONAL_ACCESS_TOKEN_MAX_TTL` (90 days). The only revocation routes (`/auth/tokens`, `/auth/devices`) are scoped to `actor.user_id` and there is no admin-side listing/revocation or user deletion, so the demoting admin cannot close the hole. The existing test `test_write_enabled_token_uses_capped_role_not_live_user_role` pins the promotion direction (token must not gain the new role) but the demotion direction was never considered.

**Evidence.** Scratch test (test_auth_probe.py::test_pat_keeps_admin_after_user_is_demoted) passed: after `PATCH /auth/users/{second}` -> role viewer, `/auth/me` with the session JWT reports viewer and `/auth/users` is 401, but with the PAT minted earlier: `GET /projects` -> 200 including root's project the user is not a member of; `POST /projects` -> 201; `DELETE /projects/{root_project}` -> 200; root's `GET /auth/tokens` returns [] (cannot see the offending token). Code: middleware.py:214-218 `AuthContext(user_id=principal.user_id, role=principal.role, principal_type=PrincipalType.SERVICE)`; auth.py:1234-1241 `role=Role(row.role)`.

**Failure scenario.** Lab member with an admin account mints a write-enabled admin PAT for an agent, then leaves the lab or is demoted to viewer by another admin. For up to 90 days the PAT (or the agent/machine holding it) can still read every project and create/delete records lab-wide; the admin who performed the demotion has no way to discover or revoke it.

**Suggested fix.** In the middleware (or in `verify_token`), compute the effective role as `_cap_role(principal.role, issuer_role=user.role)` using the freshly loaded user, so a demotion immediately narrows every token (promotion still does not widen it, preserving the existing test). Add admin routes to list and revoke any user's PATs and device tokens (and optionally revoke all credentials as part of PATCH /auth/users role/password changes).

**Verification.** verify=confirmed(high), refute=confirmed(high), lead=confirmed(high)

#### H5. Import-time Settings()/engine construction in db.py (unused SessionLocal) breaks lt, lt-mcp and the documented review-email-external worker profile

`src/lab_tracker/db.py:95` · correctness · area: config-failure-modes · also reported as runtime-config-2

`SessionLocal = get_session_factory()` runs at module import, which calls `get_settings()` (validates the full server Settings from env/.env) and `create_engine()`. `lab_tracker/__init__.py` (line 3-5) eagerly imports acquisition_watcher -> api -> auth -> db_models -> db, so ANY `import lab_tracker.<x>` (including the pure client package `lab_tracker_client`, `lab_tracker.mcp_server`, and `lab_tracker.review_email_external_worker`) constructs the server Settings before any code runs. `SessionLocal` is never referenced anywhere else (grep shows only the definition), so this is dead code with a side effect. Concretely, the documented `review-email-control` Compose profile (docker-compose.yml `review-email-control` service, docs/daily-review-email-alerts.md:82-91) sets `LAB_TRACKER_ENVIRONMENT: production` and `LAB_TRACKER_AUTH_SECRET_KEY_FILE` but not `LAB_TRACKER_AUTH_SECRET_KEY`, and overrides the entrypoint so docker-entrypoint.sh never exports the secret; `_worker_settings()` in review_email_external_worker.py:183-205 is designed to read the secret file, but the process dies on …

**Evidence.** Reproduced: `env -i LAB_TRACKER_ENVIRONMENT=production LAB_TRACKER_AUTH_SECRET_KEY_FILE=<file> LAB_TRACKER_BASE_URL=https://lab.example.org LAB_TRACKER_REVIEW_EMAIL_ENABLED=true LAB_TRACKER_REVIEW_EMAIL_TRANSPORT=external python -m lab_tracker.review_email_external_worker claim` -> traceback through lab_tracker/__init__.py:3 -> acquisition_watcher.py:13 -> api.py:12 -> auth.py:25 -> db_models.py:24 -> db.py:95 -> db.py:87 -> db.py:25 -> config.py:541: `ValidationError: LAB_TRACKER_AUTH_SECRET_KEY must be set to a strong non-placeholder value when authentication is enabled`. Same failure for `LAB_TRACKER_ENVIRONMENT=production lt --help` and `python -c 'import lab_tracker.mcp_server'`. …

**Failure scenario.** Operator follows docs/daily-review-email-alerts.md and runs `docker compose --profile review-email-external run --rm --no-deps review-email-control claim`; the container exits with a pydantic ValidationError about the auth secret and no review emails can be leased/accepted. Separately, any user whose shell/.env carries production-style LAB_TRACKER_* variables cannot run the `lt` client or `lt-mcp` at all, even …

**Suggested fix.** Delete `SessionLocal` from db.py (it is unused) and stop `lab_tracker/__init__.py` from eagerly importing the API stack (or make the client/MCP packages import only leaf modules). Add a test that imports `lab_tracker_client`, `lab_tracker.mcp_server` and the external worker in a subprocess with `LAB_TRACKER_ENVIRONMENT=production` and no secret, and a test for the worker with only `LAB_TRACKER_AUTH_SECRET_KEY_FILE` set.

**Verification.** verify=confirmed(high), refute=confirmed(high)

#### H6. Offline upload queue can never drain when authentication is disabled (default / LAN --allow-insecure-auth-disabled mode)

`src/lab_tracker/frontend_src/shared/upload-queue.js:267` · data-integrity · area: frontend-core

drainOnce() refuses to submit any queued job unless BOTH a bearer token and an ownerId are present: `if (!token || !ownerId) { results.stillQueued.push(queuedItem); continue; }`. This was written for the post-logout path, but it conflates "no token" with "no session". When LAB_TRACKER_AUTH_ENABLED is false (the documented default, and the LAN mode reachable via scripts/serve-lan.sh --allow-insecure-auth-disabled), useAuthSession keeps `token === ""` forever while /auth/me returns the local user, so app-shell.jsx:81 wires `installOfflineRetry({ getSession: () => ({ token: "", ownerId }) })` and useMobileCapture.js:183 calls `queue.drain({ token: "", ownerId })`. Every drain (boot, `online` event, post-share) short-circuits before fetch. Meanwhile uploadOrQueueRawFile happily enqueues captures in this mode and the UI tells the user "Capture queued — will upload when you're back online." (useMobileCapture.js:482). The photo/voice file sits in IndexedDB indefinitely, the PendingUploadsBadge shows "N captures queued offline" forever, and there is no UI to retry or discard. The unit test …

**Evidence.** upload-queue.js:265-270: // No active session identity -> nothing may drain. ... if (!token || !ownerId) { results.stillQueued.push(queuedItem); continue; } app-shell.jsx:81: getSession: () => ({ token: auth.token, ownerId }), // auth.token is "" when auth is disabled (useAuthSession.js:51-53, 195-198) routes/auth.py:232-239: auth-disabled /auth/me returns a user with user_id=LOCAL user (so ownerId is set, token is not). Scratch reproduction (node, real queue module, memory adapter): enqueue({ownerId: LOCAL}); drain({ token: "", ownerId: LOCAL }) -> "A auth-disabled drain -> fetch calls: 0 stillQueued: 1 uploaded: 0 pending: 1"

**Failure scenario.** Single-user or LAN deployment with auth disabled. User opens /app/capture on the phone, loses Wi-Fi, records a voice note -> app says it is queued and will upload when online. Connectivity returns; `online` fires; drain returns stillQueued without calling fetch. The note never reaches the server; badge shows "1 capture queued offline" permanently and nothing can clear it.

**Suggested fix.** Gate on session identity, not on the token: drain when `ownerId` is present and either a token exists or the app reports auth disabled (e.g. pass `{ token, ownerId, authEnabled }` from useAuthSession and treat `!authEnabled` as an authorised session). Keep the post-logout guard via `ownerId === ""` (clearSession already nulls the user). Add a test for `drain({ token: "", ownerId })` in the auth-disabled case, and surface a visible error rather …

**Verification.** verify=confirmed(high), refute=confirmed(high), lead=confirmed(medium)

#### H7. Entity IDs are interpolated unescaped into API URL paths; httpx dot-segment normalization lets any MCP tool call reach arbitrary API routes (reproduced: read tool lists /auth/users, write tool PATCHes an exploration …

`src/lab_tracker/mcp_api_client.py:558` · security · area: mcp

Every path-parameterised client method builds the request path with an f-string (`f"/goals/{goal_id}"`, `f"/projects/{project_id}/graph/overview"`, `f"/projects/{project_id}/nodes/{entity_type}/{entity_id}/goals"`, `f"/questions/{question_id}/refactor"`, `f"/claims/{claim_id}/edges"`, `f"/goals/{goal_id}/links"`, `f"/visualizations/{viz_id}/file"`, ...) without validating that the value is a UUID or percent-encoding it. httpx resolves `..` segments client-side and treats `?`/`#` as query/fragment delimiters, so an agent-supplied ID rewrites the request target. Consequences: (a) read tools carry `readOnlyHint=True` so IDEs auto-run them without confirmation, yet they can now issue any GET the MCP credential authorizes, e.g. `/auth/users`, `/auth/tokens`, `/auth/invitations` (JWT/username-password mode), `/batches`, `/graph-drafts`, `/groups`, `/notes/{id}/raw`, far outside the 'bounded graph-native' surface the hosted endpoint advertises; (b) write tools reach other mutation routes with their fixed body shape: `lab_tracker_update_goal` PATCHes `/questions/{id}` or …

**Evidence.** mcp_api_client.py:558 `return self._request("GET", f"/goals/{goal_id}")`; :368 `f"/projects/{project_id}/graph/overview"`; :1039-1041 `self._request("PATCH", f"/goals/{goal_id}", ...)`; :1079 `f"/projects/{project_id}/nodes/{entity_type}/{entity_id}/goals"`. httpx check: `httpx.Client(base_url="http://api.local").build_request("GET", "/projects/../auth/tokens").url` -> `http://api.local/auth/tokens`; `/projects/a?x=1/graph/overview` -> query becomes `x=1/graph/overview`. Scratch pytest against the real app (tests/conftest fixtures, admin JWT bridged through MockTransport): `read_tools.lab_tracker_get_goal(goal_id="../auth/users")` -> `{"data": [{"user_id": "fb6a94dc-...", "username": …

**Failure scenario.** Hosted mode: a remote agent holding only the inbound bearer calls `lab_tracker_get_goal(goal_id="../graph-drafts")` or `lab_tracker_list_node_goals(project_id="..", entity_type="batches", entity_id="runs")` and the MCP process, using the server-side LPAT, fetches routes never intended for the hosted read surface. Local stdio mode with username/password: a prompt-injected agent auto-runs …

**Suggested fix.** Validate every path-bound identifier in LabTrackerAPIClient (and ideally in the tool signatures via `Annotated[str, Field(pattern=UUID regex)]` or `uuid.UUID`) before interpolation, and percent-encode segments (`urllib.parse.quote(value, safe="")`). Constrain `layer` in export_goal_artifact/export_question_subtree to the documented literal set and `entity_type` params to the enum. Add a regression test asserting that `../`, `?` and `#` in IDs …

**Verification.** verify=confirmed(high), refute=confirmed(high), lead=confirmed(high)

#### H8. First-admin bootstrap token is served unauthenticated to any client whose TCP peer is a private address, which is every client behind a Docker-bridge or containerised reverse proxy in the default `local` disclosure mode

`src/lab_tracker/routes/auth.py:427` · security · area: sec-input · also reported as runtime-config-1, auth-5

`/auth/bootstrap-status` is in `_PUBLIC_PATHS` (app_parts/middleware.py:63) and, in the default `LAB_TRACKER_BOOTSTRAP_ADMIN_TOKEN_DISCLOSURE=local` mode, returns the plaintext bootstrap token whenever `_client_is_local(request)` is true, i.e. whenever `request.client.host` is loopback, RFC1918/private or link-local. The trust decision is made on the raw transport peer. Uvicorn only rewrites `request.client` from `X-Forwarded-For` when the peer is in `FORWARDED_ALLOW_IPS` (default `127.0.0.1` in uvicorn 0.49), and neither the Dockerfile CMD, the compose files nor any doc sets `FORWARDED_ALLOW_IPS`/`--forwarded-allow-ips`. In the root `docker-compose.yml` (LAN/self-hosted, `LAB_TRACKER_ENVIRONMENT=production`, port published on 0.0.0.0:8000, no disclosure setting) and in the `deployments/shared-provider` overlay (multi-tenant; `runtime.env.example` sets no disclosure mode) every connection that arrives via a host-level TLS proxy (nginx/Caddy -> published port; peer becomes the docker bridge gateway 172.x), a proxy container, or Docker Desktop (peer 192.168.65.1) is classified as …

**Evidence.** routes/auth.py:385-392 `host = (client.host or "")...; return address.is_loopback or address.is_private or address.is_link_local`; :427 `if _client_is_local(request): return bootstrap_token, None`. middleware.py:63 lists "/auth/bootstrap-status" as public. Dockerfile CMD: `uvicorn lab_tracker.asgi:app --host 0.0.0.0 --port 8000` (no forwarded-allow-ips); `grep -rn -i 'forwarded_allow|forwarded-allow|proxy-headers|X-Forwarded-For' docs README.md deploy` -> no matches. uvicorn.config: `self.forwarded_allow_ips = os.environ.get("FORWARDED_ALLOW_IPS", "127.0.0.1")`. Scratch test (scratchpad/p1b/test_bootstrap_peer.py) calling `_bootstrap_token_for_status` with peers ('172.18.0.1',4000), …

**Failure scenario.** Operator starts the root docker-compose stack (or shared-provider overlay) behind a Caddy/nginx TLS proxy on the same host before creating the first admin. An internet attacker GETs https://lab.example/auth/bootstrap-status, receives `bootstrap_token`, POSTs /auth/register with role=admin and that token, and becomes the instance admin with global read/write over every project.

**Suggested fix.** Do not derive trust from `request.client` unless proxy headers are explicitly configured: either default `bootstrap_admin_token_disclosure` to `never` when `environment != local`, or require an explicit trusted-proxy setting (`LAB_TRACKER_TRUSTED_PROXIES` mapped to uvicorn `forwarded_allow_ips`) before honoring private peers. Set `LAB_TRACKER_BOOTSTRAP_ADMIN_TOKEN_DISCLOSURE: never` in the root docker-compose.yml and shared-provider …

**Verification.** verify=confirmed(high), refute=confirmed(high), lead=confirmed(high)

#### H9. `git snapshot` credential stripping keeps bare-token userinfo (`https://<token>@github.com/...`) in evidence text and metadata

`src/lab_tracker_client/git_capture.py:420` · security · area: client-package

`_strip_url_credentials` only removes userinfo when it contains a `:` (user:password). GitHub's documented HTTPS-token form `https://ghp_xxx@github.com/owner/repo.git` (and the `https://${GITHUB_TOKEN}@github.com/...` pattern used in CI, which `docs/analysis-graph-drafts-ci.md` targets) has a bare token as the username and passes through untouched. The URL is then embedded in the evidence markdown (`- remote_origin:`), in `facts["git_remote_origin_url"]` (which `watch._event_metadata` copies to note metadata because it starts with `git_`), and in `source.uri` of the outbox event, all of which are uploaded to the server.

**Evidence.** git_capture.py:409-424: ``` """Drop user:password userinfo ... Bare usernames (ssh's ``git@``) are kept""" ... userinfo, _, host = parts.netloc.rpartition("@") if ":" in userinfo: return urlunsplit(...) return url ``` Scratch reproduction with remote `https://ghp_SEKRETTOKEN123@github.com/lab/repo.git`: ``` git_remote_origin_url fact: https://ghp_SEKRETTOKEN123@github.com/lab/repo.git EVIDENCE CONTAINS TOKEN: True ```

**Failure scenario.** A researcher clones with `git clone https://ghp_abc123@github.com/lab/analysis.git`, installs the `lt hooks install` post-commit hook, and commits. The commit evidence note uploaded to Lab Tracker contains the personal access token in its body and in `git_remote_origin_url` metadata.

**Suggested fix.** Strip *all* userinfo from http(s) remotes (only the scheme-less scp form `git@host:` is addressing); e.g. `if parts.scheme in {"http","https"} and "@" in parts.netloc: drop userinfo`. Share one helper between git_capture.py, repo.py and figure.py. Add a bare-token regression test.

**Verification.** verify=confirmed(high), refute=confirmed(high), lead=confirmed(high)

#### H10. `lt repo` capture records the raw remote URL, leaking embedded credentials into staged notes and metadata

`src/lab_tracker_client/repo.py:821` · security · area: client-package

`git_context()` copies `git config --get remote.origin.url` verbatim into the event's `source.repo_remote_url`. Unlike `git_capture._strip_url_credentials` and `figure._credential_free_repo_remote`, nothing strips userinfo. The value is then written to the outbox event file, rendered into the note body (`- Remote: \`...\``, repo.py:951-958) and copied into note metadata `repo_remote_url` (repo.py:1015-1016), and uploaded to the server on `lt repo sync`. Remotes of the form `https://oauth2:<token>@gitlab...` / `https://user:pass@...` are common for CI runners and `git remote set-url` with a PAT; the post-commit hook installed by `lt repo install-hook` fires on every commit, so the secret is exfiltrated to the Lab Tracker instance (shared-provider/multi-tenant included) without the user doing anything.

**Evidence.** repo.py:820-828: ``` for key, args in ( ("repo_remote_url", ("config", "--get", "remote.origin.url")), ... value = _git_output(root, *args) if value: context[key] = value ``` Scratch reproduction (repo with remote `https://oauth2:SEKRET_TOKEN@gitlab.example.com/lab/repo.git`, `capture_commit` + `render_event_note` + `event_metadata`): ``` EVENT FILE CONTAINS TOKEN: True RENDERED NOTE CONTAINS TOKEN: True METADATA repo_remote_url: https://oauth2:SEKRET_TOKEN@gitlab.example.com/lab/repo.git ``` `tests/test_repo_client.py` only covers a credential-free remote; `test_git_capture_and_hooks_cli.py::test_git_snapshot_strips_remote_credentials` covers the other adapter only.

**Failure scenario.** A CI job or a developer configures `origin` as `https://oauth2:glpat-xxxx@gitlab.example.org/lab/analysis.git`, runs `lt repo install-hook`, and commits. The staged note uploaded by `lt repo sync` contains `glpat-xxxx` in its body and in `repo_remote_url` metadata, visible to every project member and stored in the server database/backups.

**Suggested fix.** Strip userinfo in `git_context()` (reuse `git_capture._strip_url_credentials` extended to drop *any* userinfo except a bare `git` ssh user, or store only `normalize_remote(...)` in `repo_remote_url`), and add a regression test with a `user:token@` and a bare-token remote asserting the token never appears in the event file, note body or metadata.

**Verification.** verify=confirmed(high), refute=confirmed(high), lead=confirmed(high)


### MEDIUM (83)

#### M1. .env.example sets LAB_TRACKER_MCP_HOST=127.0.0.1, which makes the compose `mcp` service unreachable when the file is used as `.env`

`.env.example:155` · correctness · area: docs-drift

`.env.example` mixes host-side `lt-mcp` client settings (TRANSPORT=stdio, HOST=127.0.0.1, PORT=8000) with the compose-interpolated variables of the same names. docker-compose.yml's `mcp` service uses `LAB_TRACKER_MCP_HOST: ${LAB_TRACKER_MCP_HOST:-0.0.0.0}` with the comment "Docker needs the process reachable on the container interface". Compose reads `.env` from the project directory for interpolation, so an operator who does the conventional `cp .env.example .env` gets an MCP container that binds loopback inside its own network namespace; the published `127.0.0.1:9000->8000` port then connects to nothing. The same `.env` is also loaded by pydantic (`env_file=".env"` in Settings), so its `LAB_TRACKER_ENVIRONMENT=production` and `postgres:5432` database URL leak into every host-side `uv run pytest`, `lab-tracker`, and `lt` invocation from the repo root (deployments/shared-provider/README.md acknowledges this leakage but .env.example carries no warning).

**Evidence.** ``` $ LT_MCP_INBOUND_TOKEN=x LT_MCP_READONLY_TOKEN=y docker compose --env-file .env.example config --format json | ... mcp LAB_TRACKER_MCP_HOST = 127.0.0.1 mcp LAB_TRACKER_MCP_PORT = 8000 mcp ports = [{'mode': 'ingress', 'host_ip': '127.0.0.1', 'target': 8000, 'published': '9000', 'protocol': 'tcp'}] ``` docker-compose.yml: `LAB_TRACKER_MCP_HOST: ${LAB_TRACKER_MCP_HOST:-0.0.0.0}`; .env.example:155 `LAB_TRACKER_MCP_HOST=127.0.0.1`; src/lab_tracker/mcp_server.py:117 `host=os.getenv("LAB_TRACKER_MCP_HOST", "127.0.0.1")`.

**Failure scenario.** Operator copies .env.example to .env, runs `docker compose up mcp` per docs/deployment-options.md; the Caddy/tailscale front proxy gets connection refused on 127.0.0.1:9000 because the container process listens only on the container's loopback.

**Suggested fix.** Remove or comment out the `lt-mcp` runtime variables (LAB_TRACKER_MCP_TRANSPORT/HOST/PORT/PATH) from .env.example or move them to a clearly separate, commented-out block noting they apply only to a host-run `lt-mcp`; in compose, use a differently named interpolation variable for the container bind host (e.g. `LAB_TRACKER_MCP_CONTAINER_HOST`) so a host-side value cannot break the service. Add a header comment that `.env` is read by both Compose …

**Verification.** verify=confirmed(medium)

#### M2. Dependabot `pip` ecosystem edits pyproject.toml without regenerating uv.lock, and CI has no lock-freshness gate

`.github/dependabot.yml:11` · packaging · area: deps-supply-chain

Dependabot is configured with `package-ecosystem: "pip"`, which understands pyproject specifiers but not `uv.lock`; the `uv` ecosystem is the one that updates both. Every Dependabot Python PR therefore widens a specifier in pyproject.toml and leaves uv.lock recording the old specifier. The CI workflow cannot catch this: every job installs with `uv sync --frozen` (which deliberately ignores lock staleness) and then runs plain `uv run ...` (which silently re-locks in the CI workspace); there is no `uv lock --check` / `uv sync --locked` step, and the only drift check (`git diff --exit-code -- src/lab_tracker/frontend`, ci.yml:50) is path-restricted. This is the mechanism by which the already-known mcp `<2`/`<3` drift landed and merged green, and it will recur on the next Dependabot Python PR.

**Evidence.** `git show --stat a025e76` (dependabot[bot] "Update mcp requirement from <2,>=1.27 to >=1.27,<3 (#195)") touches only `pyproject.toml | 2 +-`; an earlier one (#128, mypy) had the same shape. `uv lock --check --offline` at HEAD prints `The lockfile at uv.lock needs to be updated`. ci.yml lines 30/36/54: `uv sync --frozen ...` followed by `uv run ruff`, `uv run mypy`, `uv run pytest` with no `--locked`/`--frozen` and no `uv lock --check` step.

**Failure scenario.** Next weekly Dependabot pip PR widens e.g. `sqlalchemy>=2.0` or bumps a lower bound; CI is green because `--frozen` installs the old lock and `uv run` re-locks in-workspace. After merge, every developer's first `uv run` rewrites uv.lock locally (dirty worktree, and `deployments/dedicated-instance/release.sh` refuses to release a dirty worktree), while the Docker image keeps installing the old locked versions — …

**Suggested fix.** Switch the Python entry to `package-ecosystem: "uv"` (Dependabot then regenerates uv.lock in the same PR). Add a CI step `uv lock --check` (or use `uv sync --locked`) before the `uv run` steps, and use `uv run --frozen`/`--no-sync` in CI so the lock is never mutated in the workspace.

**Verification.** verify=confirmed(medium)

#### M3. restore-smoke readiness probe uses the Unix socket and can pass during the postgres image's init-only temporary server, making the single-shot TCP pg_restore fail

`deployments/dedicated-instance/restore-smoke.sh:89` · correctness · area: deploy-packaging

The scratch Postgres is always a first-boot container (fresh volume). The official image starts a temporary server with `listen_addresses=''` (Unix socket only) while it creates the database/user, stops it, and then starts the real server on TCP. `docker exec ... pg_isready -U ... -d ...` with no `-h` checks the Unix socket, so it can succeed during the temporary phase. The very next step is a one-shot `pg_restore --host postgres ... --exit-on-error` over TCP from another container with no retry; if it lands in the temp-server window or the restart gap it fails with connection refused / 'the database system is starting up', the smoke exits 1, and release.sh aborts before cutover. The same socket-only pg_isready pattern is used in the compose healthchecks (docker-compose.yml:124, dedicated docker-compose.yml:53), where the entrypoint's 3x2s migration retry masks it.

**Evidence.** restore-smoke.sh:88-98 loops on `docker exec "${postgres_container}" pg_isready -U ... -d ...` (no -h, socket), then 100-112 runs `pg_restore --host postgres ... --exit-on-error` once with no retry. No `docker run` here is a re-used volume, so init-phase behaviour always applies.

**Failure scenario.** Operator runs `deployments/dedicated-instance/release.sh`; backup succeeds, restore-smoke's pg_isready returns 0 during the init temp server, pg_restore over TCP is refused, release.sh exits 1 with 'Disposable restore' failure although the backup is valid. Repeating the command usually works, which erodes trust in the gate.

**Suggested fix.** Probe over TCP from the network the restore will use (e.g. `docker run --rm --network $network postgres:16-alpine pg_isready -h postgres -U ...`) or add `-h 127.0.0.1` to the exec'd pg_isready, and/or retry pg_restore's connection a few times.

**Verification.** verify=confirmed(medium)

#### M4. mcp compose service inherits the Dockerfile HEALTHCHECK on /health, which the MCP ASGI app answers with 401, so the container is permanently unhealthy

`docker-compose.yml:55` · correctness · area: deploy-packaging

Dockerfile:41 declares `HEALTHCHECK ... urlopen('http://127.0.0.1:8000/health')`. The root-compose `mcp` service reuses that image and does not override the healthcheck, but the streamable-HTTP app is `MCPInboundBearerAuthMiddleware(FastMCP.streamable_http_app())`: every request without the inbound bearer gets 401 and `/health` does not exist even with the bearer (404). `urllib.request.urlopen` raises on both, so the health probe fails on every tick and `docker compose ps` reports the service as unhealthy; anything with `depends_on: mcp: condition: service_healthy` would never start. The shared-provider overlay silently papers over this with a TCP-socket healthcheck, which confirms the mismatch was noticed but the base file was not fixed.

**Evidence.** Scratch test building the same app as `_run_streamable_http`: `GET /health -> 401` (no bearer) and `GET /health (bearer) -> 404 Not Found`. Dockerfile:41-42 HEALTHCHECK uses urlopen on /health; docker-compose.yml mcp service (lines 55-75) has no `healthcheck:` override; deployments/shared-provider/docker-compose.yml:16-25 overrides it with a socket probe.

**Failure scenario.** `docker compose up mcp` per docs/deployment-options.md:59 starts, serves MCP correctly, but Docker marks the container `unhealthy` after start_period; operators or orchestration tooling that key on container health (or a future `depends_on` on mcp) treat the service as down.

**Suggested fix.** Add the socket-based healthcheck (or a real unauthenticated `/health` route in the MCP ASGI app) to the root compose `mcp` service, or set `healthcheck: disable: true` there.

**Verification.** verify=confirmed(medium)

#### M5. compose `mcp` service re-resolves and reinstalls the project with `uv run` at container start instead of using the frozen image env

`docker-compose.yml:59` · packaging · area: deps-supply-chain · also reported as deploy-packaging-5

The Dockerfile builds a reproducible environment with `uv sync --frozen --no-dev --no-editable` (Dockerfile:30), but the `mcp` service overrides the command with `uv run python -m lab_tracker.mcp_server`. Plain `uv run` is not a passive launcher: it (1) checks/re-locks `uv.lock` against `pyproject.toml` and (2) syncs the project, which means rebuilding `lab-tracker` from `/app/src` as an *editable* install (uv's default) — replacing the wheel that was installed at image build time. Because the runtime user `labtracker` has an empty uv cache (the build ran as root), this needs PyPI access at every container start (to fetch `setuptools`/`wheel` build deps and, given the current mcp constraint drift, to re-resolve the whole graph) and rewrites `/app/uv.lock` inside the container. The mode is inherited by `deployments/shared-provider/docker-compose.yml` (the overlay pins the image but keeps the base `command`), so the multi-tenant hosted-MCP mode has a runtime that is not the reviewed immutable image. The sibling `review-email-control` service (line 100-103) already does this correctly …

**Evidence.** Simulated the image steps in scratch: after `uv sync --frozen --no-dev --no-editable`, running `UV_CACHE_DIR=<empty> uv run --offline python -c 'import lab_tracker'` fails with `Packages were unavailable because the network was disabled` (first because the lock is stale, and — after making pyproject match the lock — still fails: `setuptools>=61.0 ... unsatisfiable`, i.e. it needs the build backend to re-install the project editable). With network, the same command prints `Building lab-tracker @ file:///... Uninstalled 1 package / Installed 1 package` and site-packages then contains `__editable__.lab_tracker-0.1.0.pth`. `uv run --no-sync --offline python -c 'import lab_tracker.mcp_server'` …

**Failure scenario.** Operator follows docs/deployment-options.md / docs/lab-tracker-mcp-skills.md (`docker compose up mcp`) on a host without outbound PyPI access, or PyPI is down: the mcp container never starts (uv resolution error) even though the image contains everything needed. With network, the container silently re-resolves dependencies at boot and runs an editable install built from /app/src, so the code and dependency set that …

**Suggested fix.** Change the command to `python -m lab_tracker.mcp_server` (or `lt-mcp`), matching the `review-email-control` service, and add an assertion in tests/test_mcp_server.py's compose test that the mcp command does not start with `uv run`. If uv must be used, `uv run --frozen --no-sync` is the passive form.

**Verification.** verify=confirmed(medium)

#### M6. First-admin (non-Docker) setup instructions fail at startup: auth enabled without a signing secret is rejected

`docs/setup.md:242` · docs-drift · area: docs-drift

docs/setup.md:242-244 tells the operator to `export LAB_TRACKER_AUTH_ENABLED=true`, set `LAB_TRACKER_BOOTSTRAP_ADMIN_TOKEN`, and run `lab-tracker serve`. `Settings._validate_auth_secret_key` (src/lab_tracker/config.py) rejects the default secret whenever auth is enabled, including in `local`, so this exact sequence aborts before serving. The same omission appears in docs/configuration.md:877 ("Set `LAB_TRACKER_AUTH_ENABLED=true` to test the login and role flow") and docs/lab-tracker-mcp-skills.md ("Set `LAB_TRACKER_AUTH_ENABLED=true` when you want to test login"). docs/configuration.md:51 is also wrong: the default secret is not "allowed only in `local`"; it is allowed only while auth is disabled.

**Evidence.** ``` $ uv run --no-sync python -c "from lab_tracker.config import Settings; Settings(_env_file=None, environment='local', auth_enabled=True, bootstrap_admin_token='one-time-admin-token')" 1 validation error for Settings Value error, LAB_TRACKER_AUTH_SECRET_KEY must be set to a strong non-placeholder value when authentication is enabled. ``` config.py: `if self.is_auth_enabled() and auth_secret_key in INSECURE_AUTH_SECRET_KEYS: raise ValueError(...)` with `auth_secret_key: str = DEFAULT_AUTH_SECRET_KEY`. docs/lan-shared-graph.md does set the secret, so only the setup/configuration/mcp-skills pages are wrong.

**Failure scenario.** An IT contact follows the 'Non-Docker' first-admin section verbatim; `lab-tracker serve` exits with a pydantic ValidationError instead of opening the Create First Admin screen. (It fails loud, so no security impact, but the documented workflow does not work.)

**Suggested fix.** Add `export LAB_TRACKER_AUTH_SECRET_KEY="<long-random-secret>"` to setup.md:242, configuration.md:877, and lab-tracker-mcp-skills.md; correct configuration.md:51 to say the placeholder default is rejected whenever authentication is enabled.

**Verification.** verify=confirmed(medium), refute=confirmed(medium), lead=confirmed(medium)

#### M7. Hosted GitHub Pages demo breaks on any nested route: 404.html uses relative asset URLs

`scripts/build-pages-demo.mjs:80` · correctness · area: frontend-build-tests

The demo builder writes the same `appHtml` to `app/index.html` and to the site-root `404.html`, and `appHtml` references its assets with document-relative URLs (`static/styles.css`, `static/app.js?v=...`, lines 64-70). GitHub Pages serves `404.html` at the original request URL, so for any route deeper than `/app/<one-segment>` (question/dataset/session/batch detail pages, which the SPA navigates to with pushState) the browser resolves `static/app.js` under the route path and gets the 404 HTML page back as the script. Refreshing, sharing or bookmarking any detail page of the hosted demo therefore renders nothing. The docs (docs/deployment-options.md:93-99) call this demo 'the primary non-build preview path'.

**Evidence.** Live probe: `curl https://samuelbrudner.github.io/lab-tracker/app/questions/6e3bfd66-ce26-4a66-ab08-1b951bb79d56` -> status=404, body is the demo shell with `href="static/styles.css?v=18"` / `src="static/app.js..."`; `curl https://samuelbrudner.github.io/lab-tracker/app/questions/static/app.js` -> status=404, content-type text/html (the 404 page), i.e. the script tag loads HTML. The root `/lab-tracker/app/` loads fine (status=200). The current script reproduces the same structure: `await writeFile(resolve(outDir, "404.html"), appHtml, "utf-8")` at line 80 with relative hrefs at lines 64-70.

**Failure scenario.** A reader clicks the README's read-only demo link, opens a question detail (URL becomes /lab-tracker/app/questions/<id>), then refreshes or shares the link. GitHub Pages serves 404.html; `static/app.js` resolves to /lab-tracker/app/questions/static/app.js, which returns the 404 HTML; the app never mounts and the page is blank apart from the noscript text.

**Suggested fix.** Emit root-absolute asset URLs in the demo shell (accept a base path argument, e.g. `/lab-tracker/app/`, and render `<base href>` or `${basePath}static/...`), or make 404.html a tiny redirect that preserves the path (e.g. sessionStorage-redirect pattern) instead of a copy of the app shell. Add a build-time assertion/test that 404.html asset URLs are absolute.

**Verification.** verify=confirmed(medium)

#### M8. Windows daily-review scheduled task has no way to carry credentials, so the documented auth-enabled flow on Windows fails silently

`scripts/install-daily-review.ps1:33` · docs-drift · area: cli-ops · also reported as review-email-scheduling-9

docs/scheduled-daily-review.md ("Running against a server (with login)") tells operators to set LAB_TRACKER_API_KEY / LAB_TRACKER_ADMIN_USER+PASS and "Pass a non-local URL to the installer with -BaseUrl (Windows)". The *nix installers persist the exported secret to a 0600 JSON file that the trigger reads back; the PowerShell installer does not persist anything: the task action is only `-File daily-review-run-due.ps1 -BaseUrl <url>`, and the trigger reads `$env:LAB_TRACKER_API_KEY` at run time. A Task Scheduler process does not inherit a `$env:` assignment made in the installing PowerShell session, so on an auth-enabled server every scheduled firing hits `/batches/run-due` with no Authorization header, `Invoke-RestMethod` throws on the 401, and the task's failure is invisible (no log redirection). The .ps1 help even says "The default targets a local Lab Tracker with auth disabled", but the docs present -BaseUrl as the Windows path to a deployed server.

**Evidence.** scripts/install-daily-review.ps1:33-34: $action = New-ScheduledTaskAction -Execute "powershell.exe" ` -Argument "-NoProfile -ExecutionPolicy Bypass -File `"$trigger`" -BaseUrl `"$BaseUrl`"" scripts/daily-review-run-due.ps1:16-18: [string]$ApiKey = $env:LAB_TRACKER_API_KEY, ... docs/scheduled-daily-review.md:159-160: "Pass a non-local URL to the installer with `-BaseUrl` (Windows) or as the second argument" (in the auth-enabled section). No Windows equivalent of write-secrets/read-secret exists (scheduler_install.py write_secrets_file uses O_NOFOLLOW, *nix only).

**Failure scenario.** A Windows lab PC is set up to poll a hosted, auth-enabled Lab Tracker: `$env:LAB_TRACKER_API_KEY='lpat_…'; scripts/install-daily-review.ps1 -BaseUrl https://lab.example.org`. The task registers fine, but every run 401s and no daily review is ever drafted; there is no error the operator can see.

**Suggested fix.** Either persist the credential on Windows too (e.g. write it to a per-user file under %LOCALAPPDATA% with a restrictive ACL and pass the path via the task arguments, mirroring the *nix design) or have the installer refuse/warn when -BaseUrl is non-local and no persisted credential exists, and document that Windows requires a persistent user-level environment variable (setx) rather than `$env:`.

**Verification.** verify=confirmed(medium)

#### M9. Re-running the cron/launchd daily-review installer without the secret exported silently wipes the persisted credentials file

`scripts/install-daily-review.sh:80` · data-integrity · area: cli-ops

Both *nix installers are documented as idempotent ("Re-running it just updates the existing job", docs/scheduled-daily-review.md:75-76) and are the documented way to change the interval or base URL. On every run they call `scheduler_install.py write-secrets` with whatever is in the current environment. `write_secrets_file` drops empty values and opens the file with O_TRUNC, so a re-run from a shell where `LAB_TRACKER_API_KEY` / admin creds are not exported replaces a previously persisted token with `{}`. Nothing warns; the next cron/launchd firing then POSTs `/batches/run-due` unauthenticated, gets 401, and the failure only shows up in the log file nobody reads. The same sequence exists in install-daily-review-launchd.sh:67-70.

**Evidence.** scripts/install-daily-review.sh:80-83 (unconditional): LAB_TRACKER_API_KEY="${LAB_TRACKER_API_KEY:-}" ... "$PYTHON" "$SCHED" write-secrets "$SECRETS_FILE" scripts/scheduler_install.py:237-238: data = {key: value for key, value in values.items() if value} fd = os.open(path, os.O_WRONLY | os.O_CREAT | os.O_TRUNC | os.O_NOFOLLOW, 0o600) Reproduced with a stub crontab on PATH and HOME in scratch: 1st run with LAB_TRACKER_API_KEY=lpat_persisted -> secrets file: {"LAB_TRACKER_API_KEY": "lpat_persisted"} 2nd run `install-daily-review.sh 30 http://127.0.0.1:8000` (no token in env) -> secrets file: {}

**Failure scenario.** An operator installs the scheduler with a batch_run_due token exported, later re-runs `scripts/install-daily-review.sh 30` in a fresh terminal to change the interval. The token is erased; the daily review stops firing on the auth-enabled server with no visible error.

**Suggested fix.** Only rewrite the secrets file when at least one credential variable is set in the environment; otherwise keep the existing file (and say so in the summary). At minimum print a loud warning when an existing non-empty secrets file is about to be replaced by an empty one.

**Verification.** verify=confirmed(medium)

#### M10. serve-lan.sh auth-disabled guard only fires for 0.0.0.0/::, so `--host <LAN IP>` serves the graph unauthenticated on the network

`scripts/serve-lan.sh:84` · security · area: cli-ops

The helper documents (docs/lan-shared-graph.md, docs/setup.md) that it "refuses to bind while authentication is disabled" unless `--allow-insecure-auth-disabled` is passed. The guard is implemented as `if [ "$HOST" = "0.0.0.0" ] || [ "$HOST" = "::" ]`, but the script also accepts `--host <addr>`. Any explicit non-loopback address skips the auth evaluation entirely and goes straight to `alembic upgrade head` + `uvicorn --host <addr>`. The Python launcher (`lab_tracker.cli.serve_app`) gets this right with `_is_non_loopback_host`; the shell helper does not. The PowerShell helper hard-codes 0.0.0.0 so it is not affected.

**Evidence.** scripts/serve-lan.sh:84-101: if [ "$HOST" = "0.0.0.0" ] || [ "$HOST" = "::" ]; then AUTH_ENABLED=... if [ "$AUTH_ENABLED" != "true" ] && [ "$ALLOW_INSECURE_AUTH_DISABLED" -ne 1 ]; then echo "Refusing to bind ..."; exit 1 Reproduced with the repo venv and auth disabled (default local settings): $ sh scripts/serve-lan.sh -> "Refusing to bind Lab Tracker to 0.0.0.0 while authentication is disabled." $ sh scripts/serve-lan.sh --host 10.0.0.5 -> ran migrations, then uvicorn: "Started server process ... could not bind on any address out of [('10.0.0.5', 8000)]" (no refusal; on a host that owns that address it would listen).

**Failure scenario.** A lab member with auth disabled runs `scripts/serve-lan.sh --host 192.168.1.20` (a natural thing to do on a multi-homed box). The helper starts an unauthenticated Lab Tracker on the LAN with full write access for anyone who can reach the port, despite the documented refusal.

**Suggested fix.** Apply the auth check whenever HOST is not a loopback address (mirror `_is_non_loopback_host`: anything other than 127.0.0.1/::1/localhost, or use python `ipaddress.ip_address(host).is_loopback`). Alternatively drop the `--host` option and delegate to `lab-tracker serve --host ... --insecure-allow-lan`, which already has the guard.

**Verification.** verify=confirmed(medium)

#### M11. A single dangling symlink anywhere under a recovery root aborts the whole content-hash recovery scan (both helpers)

`src/lab_tracker/_local_store_health_helper.py:1040` · correctness · area: subprocess-platform

During enumerate-files / enumerate-registered-files, a symlink entry is re-resolved through _resolve_enumeration_components. Only _StaticEnumerationSkip is treated as 'skip this entry' (line 1774 in _classify_enumeration_entry). When the link target does not exist, _lstat_component(..., missing=True) at line 1040 raises _Missing, which propagates out of _scan_enumeration_directory and _enumerate_files unchanged, so main() returns MISSING_EXIT (4) with no JSON. The parent (_parse_enumeration_result) treats any non-zero exit as FAILED, artifact_resolution.py:1895 then calls budget.abort_terminal() and returns _RecoveryDisposition.FAILED, so recovery is disabled for that resolution. The Windows helper behaves the same: _resolve_enumeration_file_target converts WindowsLocalStoreHealthMissing to WindowsLocalStoreHealthError at line 2222 (dangling file symlink), and a dangling directory symlink raises Missing from open_enumerable_entry (line 3097) which _execute_enumeration_request maps to FAILED_EXIT. A broken link is a static filesystem condition, not a race, and it is common in lab …

**Evidence.** Scratch test (scratchpad/q4/test_enum_dangling.py) running the real POSIX helper: root containing a.txt plus `broken -> does-not-exist` returns exit 4 with empty stdout for both enumerate-files and enumerate-registered-files (baseline without the link returns 0 with a.txt as a candidate). Scratch test using the repo's ScriptedEnumerationApi (scratchpad/q4/test_win_dangling.py) against the Windows helper: a relative file symlink to `gone.bin` and a directory symlink to `gone-dir` both return FAILED_EXIT (3) with no output, discarding the sibling keep.bin candidate.

**Failure scenario.** Operator enables LAB_TRACKER_RESOLVER_RECOVERY; a moved artifact would be recoverable, but one stale symlink anywhere under any allowed root (e.g. an old `latest -> run-0042` link) makes every recovery attempt return FAILED and terminally consume the resolution budget, so recovery never works on that server and the failure is opaque (path-free FAILED).

**Suggested fix.** In _resolve_enumeration_components (and its Windows counterpart _resolve_enumeration_file_target / open_enumerable_entry), treat a missing component encountered while resolving a *listed symlink's target* as a static skip (raise _StaticEnumerationSkip / return None) instead of propagating _Missing/Error, keeping the identity-mismatch checks that genuinely indicate a race as fatal. Add POSIX and Windows tests asserting that a dangling link is …

**Verification.** verify=confirmed(medium)

#### M12. An unreadable subdirectory anywhere under a recovery root makes the whole enumeration DENIED

`src/lab_tracker/_local_store_health_helper.py:1114` · correctness · area: subprocess-platform

Directory entries are queued and later re-resolved by _traverse_enumeration_boundary through _resolve_enumeration_components, which opens each component with _open_directory (line 1114). EACCES/EPERM is in _DENIED_ERRNOS, so the open raises _Denied; _traverse_enumeration_boundary only converts _StaticEnumerationSkip (line 1899) and _Denied propagates to main() as DENIED_EXIT (2). The parent maps it to FAILED and terminally aborts the budget. On shared lab servers a mode-700 directory owned by another user under an allowed root is normal (per-user scratch dirs, instrument service accounts), so recovery is effectively unusable there. The Windows helper has the same shape: open_enumerable_entry raises WindowsLocalStoreHealthDenied on STATUS_ACCESS_DENIED and _execute_enumeration_request returns DENIED_EXIT for the whole request. Note docs/configuration.md:229-234 explicitly aims to avoid 'turning every recovery into an opaque runtime failure' at startup, but runtime does exactly that for these static conditions.

**Evidence.** Scratch test (scratchpad/q4/test_enum_eacces.py) monkeypatches os.open to raise PermissionError(EACCES) for the `private` component only (the sandbox runs as uid 0 so a real chmod cannot produce EACCES); helper.main returns 2 for a root containing a.txt and private/b.txt, i.e. a.txt is not reported. The repo's own test_native_directory_search_permission_contract is skipped as root and covers inspect-directory only, not enumeration.

**Failure scenario.** LAB_TRACKER_RESOLVER_ALLOWED_ROOTS=/data with recovery enabled; /data/alice is 0700 owned by another account. Every recovery attempt for any project fails with an opaque FAILED and the budget is consumed, even though the artifact sits in a readable sibling directory.

**Suggested fix.** Treat EACCES/EPERM (and ENOENT) on a queued child directory as a skip that sets directory_limited/candidate_limited (status 'limit') rather than aborting, reserving DENIED/FAILED for the root/store boundary and for identity mismatches that indicate a race. Mirror the same policy in the Windows enumerator and add tests for both platforms.

**Verification.** verify=confirmed(medium)

#### M13. AcquisitionOutputWatcher swallows every registration error silently and retries forever without logging

`src/lab_tracker/acquisition_watcher.py:102` · error-handling · area: file-storage

`scan()` wraps `register_acquisition_output` in `except Exception:` and only records a retry timestamp. There is no logging, no counter, no way for the caller to learn that outputs are not being registered. A persistent failure (wrong or deleted session id -> NotFoundError, actor without contributor rights -> AuthError, misconfigured DB) produces a watcher that runs indefinitely, re-hashing and re-submitting the same file every `failure_backoff_seconds`, while `scan()` returns `[]` exactly as it would for an idle directory. This is the exported public class (`lab_tracker.AcquisitionOutputWatcher`) and contradicts the project's fail-loud rule. The existing test only asserts that the watcher continues past a failing file, not that anything is surfaced.

**Evidence.** acquisition_watcher.py:94-104: `try: output = self._api.register_acquisition_output(...) except Exception: self._retry_after[file_path] = now + self._failure_backoff_seconds; continue`. Scratch test run1/test_p4_repro.py::test_watcher_swallows_register_errors_without_logging used an API stub raising NotFoundError('Session does not exist.') with failure_backoff_seconds=0: two scans returned `[]` and `caplog.records == []` at DEBUG level — passed.

**Failure scenario.** Operator starts the watcher against a session UUID that was later deleted (or with an actor lacking contributor rights). Instrument writes 500 files; none are registered; `scan()` keeps returning [] and the process appears healthy. The session is later promoted to a dataset with no outputs.

**Suggested fix.** Log at WARNING with the file path and exception (`_logger.warning(..., exc_info=True)`), distinguish non-retryable errors (NotFoundError/AuthError/ValidationError) from transient ones and raise or stop on the former, and expose a failure count or last-error on the watcher so `run()` callers can alert.

**Verification.** verify=confirmed(medium)

#### M14. SQLite migrations are not atomic: a failed migration leaves half-applied DDL and a stale _alembic_tmp table behind with alembic_version unchanged

`src/lab_tracker/alembic/env.py:61` · error-handling · area: db-models-migrations

env.py runs SQLite with transactional_ddl=False and the engine pins pysqlite legacy transaction control (db.py:59-70), under which DDL statements execute in autocommit and only INSERT/UPDATE/DELETE open a transaction. Alembic's per-migration transaction therefore only covers statements after the first DML. When a migration fails mid-way (e.g. 0035's DROP TABLE questions blocked by a NO ACTION FK), the ALTER TABLE ... ADD COLUMN and CREATE TABLE _alembic_tmp_* already executed persist, while version_num stays at the previous revision. Re-running `alembic upgrade head` then fails on 'duplicate column name' / existing tmp table, and the operator has to hand-repair the schema. 0056 and 0058 partially acknowledge this with a manual `BEGIN IMMEDIATE`, but every other migration is exposed.

**Evidence.** scratchpad/q1/cascade3.py G_0034_to_0035_with_dataset: err=IntegrityError ... version_num=0034_terminal_transition_reasons, `claims has created_by=True` (column from 0035 line 67 persisted), tmp_tables=['_alembic_tmp_claims'].

**Failure scenario.** Any SQLite migration that raises after its first DDL (constraint violation, preflight RuntimeError placed after DDL, disk error) leaves the database in a state neither revision describes; the documented recovery command (`alembic upgrade head`) fails on the residue.

**Suggested fix.** Begin an explicit physical transaction per migration on SQLite (e.g. `connection.exec_driver_sql('BEGIN IMMEDIATE')` in env.py before each migration via the `on_version_apply`/`begin_transaction` hook, or set the sqlite3 connection to PEP-249 mode for the Alembic engine) so DDL, data backfills and the version stamp commit or roll back together; keep preflight checks before any DDL.

**Verification.** verify=confirmed(medium)

#### M15. Per-migration `PRAGMA foreign_keys=ON` re-enable is a silent no-op, leaving FK enforcement off for the rest of the run and making migration outcome depend on the starting revision

`src/lab_tracker/alembic/versions/0026_project_groups.py:89` · data-integrity · area: db-models-migrations

0026, 0039, 0040, 0043, 0046_curation, 0051, 0052, 0054, 0056 and 0060(downgrade) wrap batch operations in `PRAGMA foreign_keys=OFF ... finally PRAGMA foreign_keys=ON`. SQLite ignores this pragma while a transaction is open, and by the time the `finally` runs the batch copy's INSERT (or a preceding UPDATE) has opened pysqlite's implicit transaction, so the ON never takes effect; the connection stays with foreign keys disabled until Alembic disconnects. Consequences: (1) every later migration in the same run executes without FK enforcement, including data backfills (0028, 0041, 0054/0056 preflights) whose correctness the authors assumed FKs would police; (2) the cascade hazard in the companion finding is masked or exposed purely by which revision the DB starts from, which is why the existing tests (which start at 0038/0039 and hit a toggling migration first) pass while a 0056-0060 start loses data.

**Evidence.** Direct sqlite3 check in scratchpad/q1/cascade2.py: `PRAGMA foreign_keys after OFF-inside-txn: 1 in_transaction= True` (no-op) vs `after OFF-outside-txn: 0`. Chain-level confirmation: F_0055_to_head (0056 toggles first) -> graph_change_operations before=1 after=1 (0061 did NOT cascade because FK was left OFF), whereas B2_0056_to_head (no toggle before 0061) -> after=0. The docstring in 0039 lines 92-95 states the pragma is honored because transactional_ddl=False, which is only true for the OFF half.

**Failure scenario.** Operator upgrades a SQLite DB from 0038 to head: 0039 turns FKs off, the re-enable in its finally block is ignored, and 0041's DELETE and 0054/0056 preflights run with referential checks disabled; if any migration after that inserts or updates FK columns with stale ids, SQLite accepts them silently. Conversely a DB at 0057 hits 0061 with FKs on and loses data. Same code, opposite outcomes.

**Suggested fix.** Toggle the pragma once per run in env.py outside any transaction (or via `op.get_context().autocommit_block()`), and assert `PRAGMA foreign_keys` state in a scratch assertion after each toggle so a no-op fails loudly. Delete the per-migration copies of `_set_sqlite_foreign_keys`.

**Verification.** verify=confirmed(medium)

#### M16. DELETE /projects/{id} always loses its usage-telemetry event because the after-commit insert violates the usage_events.project_id FK

`src/lab_tracker/api_parts/projects.py:106` · correctness · area: application-layer

`ProjectsApiMixin.delete_project` records its usage event with `project_id=project_id`. `BaseService.record_usage_event` persists OK-outcome events in an after-commit action (`persist_event` in services/base.py:345-352) that runs only after the request transaction has committed the cascade delete. `UsageEventModel.project_id` carries `ForeignKey("projects.project_id", ondelete="SET NULL")` (db_models.py:1751-1754), so the INSERT references a project that no longer exists and fails with an IntegrityError on both SQLite (PRAGMA foreign_keys=ON) and Postgres. `_run_deferred_actions` swallows it as a warning, so every project deletion silently drops its DELETE PROJECT telemetry row and emits a 'Deferred after_commit action failed' warning. The same pattern would affect any future delete whose usage event carries the id of the row being deleted.

**Evidence.** Scratch test (LAB_TRACKER_USAGE_EVENTS=true, real app + TestClient): after POST /projects then DELETE /projects/{id} (200), usage_events contains only [('create','project')] and `lab_tracker.api._logger.warning` was called with: "Deferred after_commit action failed: (sqlite3.IntegrityError) FOREIGN KEY constraint failed [SQL: INSERT INTO usage_events (... project_id ...) VALUES (...)] [parameters: (..., 'delete', 'project', 'f98987f2-...', ..., 'f98987f2-...', 'ok', 27, None)]". Code path: api.py:464-471 -> services/base.py:345-364 (`run_after_commit(persist_event)`) -> request_context.py:88-91 runs it after `repository.commit()` in api.py:517.

**Failure scenario.** Any operator with usage telemetry enabled deletes a project via the API/PWA/CLI. The delete succeeds, but the DELETE PROJECT usage event is never stored and a warning with the full SQL statement is logged on every deletion; usage summaries/rollups under-count deletions.

**Suggested fix.** Either persist delete events with `project_id=None` for the deleted project (keep the id in `resource_id`, which has no FK), or record delete-verb events inside the same transaction before the row is removed, or drop the FK on usage_events.project_id since telemetry rows are intentionally decoupled from domain rows. Add a regression test asserting the DELETE PROJECT event exists after deletion.

**Verification.** verify=confirmed(medium)

#### M17. App starts and reports /health OK against an unmigrated or stale database; the only schema check is downgraded to a warning

`src/lab_tracker/app_parts/runtime.py:281` · error-handling · area: config-failure-modes · also reported as lead-4

`_build_app_runtime` performs no Alembic revision check. The single DB touch at startup (`ensure_local_auth_user`) catches `SQLAlchemyError` and logs `Local auth user bootstrap skipped: ...` as a warning, then continues. The Docker HEALTHCHECK, `render.yaml healthCheckPath` and the dedicated-instance compose healthcheck all use `/health`, which never touches the database, so a process with a missing/old schema is reported healthy while every data route returns 500. The migration-head check exists only in `deployment_probe.py` (release automation), not in the running app. Docs that run uvicorn directly (docs/configuration.md:920-921, docs/lab-tracker-mcp-skills.md:209-210, docs/lan-shared-graph.md:62) rely on the operator remembering `alembic upgrade head` first, and `deploy/docker-entrypoint.sh` only covers the container path.

**Evidence.** Reproduced with a fresh SQLite file and `create_app()`: startup log `WARNING Local auth user bootstrap skipped: (sqlite3.OperationalError) no such table: users`; then `GET /health -> 200 {"status":"ok"}`, `GET /readiness -> 503`, `GET /projects -> 500` with `Unhandled HTTP exception ... sqlite3.OperationalError: no such table: projects`. No test references `ensure_local_auth_user` or 'no such table'.

**Failure scenario.** A LAN-shared or self-managed instance is started with `uvicorn lab_tracker.asgi:app` after a `git pull` that added migrations (or pointed at the wrong DB file). Uptime probes stay green, the SPA loads, and every API call 500s until someone reads the logs; in auth-disabled mode the local user row is silently absent so device pairing and attribution FKs later fail.

**Suggested fix.** At startup compare `alembic_version` against `ScriptDirectory.get_heads()` (reuse deployment_probe._expected_migration_heads) and raise if they differ or the table is missing; re-raise from `ensure_local_auth_user` instead of warning. Consider making /health depend on a cheap `SELECT 1` so orchestrators see the failure.

**Verification.** verify=confirmed(medium), lead=confirmed(low)

#### M18. Multipart file uploads are fully received and spooled to disk before any size limit runs; chunked uploads bypass the Content-Length pre-check entirely

`src/lab_tracker/application/file_commands.py:257` · performance · area: file-storage · also reported as sec-input-3

The upload routes (`/datasets/{id}/files`, `/visualizations/{id}/file`, `/notes/upload-file`, `/notes/quick-capture`) declare `file: Annotated[UploadFile, File()]`. FastAPI resolves that dependency by calling `request.form()` before the handler body runs, and Starlette's MultiPartParser writes file parts into a `SpooledTemporaryFile` with no size cap (`max_part_size` only applies to non-file fields; see .venv/.../starlette/formparsers.py lines 184-185 vs 230). Only after the whole body is on disk does the handler call `_enforce_content_length_limit(raw_content_length, ...)` / `enforce_request_content_length_limit(...)`, whose docstring claims to 'Reject obviously oversized uploads before reading the body'. If the client omits Content-Length (Transfer-Encoding: chunked) the pre-check is a no-op and the only guard is `enforce_stream_size_limit` inside `store_stream`, which runs after the full upload has been received and spooled. `max_upload_bytes` therefore bounds persistent storage, not request ingestion. No deployment asset (Dockerfile, docker-compose, render.yaml, deployments/*) …

**Evidence.** Scratch test (run1/test_p4_repro.py::test_chunked_multipart_is_fully_spooled_before_any_limit_check) set max_upload_bytes=1024 and posted a 3 MiB multipart body via a generator (chunked, no Content-Length). Output: `status 413 {'error': {'code': 'payload_too_large', ...}}` and `seen {'spooled_bytes': 3145728, 'content_length_header': None}` — the handler received all 3,145,728 bytes before the limit rejected the request. Code: file_commands.py:257-260 `_enforce_content_length_limit(raw_content_length, max_bytes=self.settings.max_upload_bytes)` runs inside `upload()`, i.e. after `File()` parsing; upload_security.py:68-85 returns silently when the header is absent.

**Failure scenario.** Authenticated user sends `POST /datasets/{id}/files` with Transfer-Encoding: chunked and a multi-GB body (or repeats 100 MiB uploads in parallel). Starlette spools everything to /tmp before the app sees the request; disk fills, other uploads and SQLite writes on the same volume fail. The 413 is returned only after the whole body was consumed.

**Suggested fix.** Enforce ingestion limits before parsing: either an ASGI middleware that rejects requests with Content-Length > max_upload_bytes and wraps `receive` to abort once more than max_upload_bytes have been read, or stop using `File()` on upload routes and stream `request.stream()` through `python-multipart` manually with a byte budget. Also pass `max_files=1` to form parsing. Document a proxy body limit in deployments/*. Fix the misleading docstring on …

**Verification.** verify=confirmed(medium)

`src/lab_tracker/application/store_health_queries.py:26` · docs-drift · area: store-authority

StoreHealthQueries.check only detaches a binding when store.kind is in _REMOTE_HEALTH_STORE_KINDS = {HTTP, GIT, rclone kinds}. Every local_fs store therefore returns StoreHealth(UNSUPPORTED, 'Store health probe is temporarily unavailable.') before the checker is ever called. The runtime still composes LocalStoreHealthProbe into _StoreHealthDispatchProbe (app_parts/runtime.py:326) and BoundedLocalFilesystemOperations.inspect_directory exists solely to serve it, but no production path can reach them. docs/retained-v1-surface.md:190-192 states 'Registered local-store health ... receive those exact shared objects' and :216 'Local health remains an isolated, output-free, point-in-time advisory operation', and docs/data-store-registry-design.md:397-411 lists local health as shipped, while the same design doc at :130-134 says local use 'must produce an opaque I/O denial until the local-use slice retains the selected grant'. The docs contradict each other and the retained surface (which CLAUDE.md says wins) describes a workflow the code does not provide. The wording 'temporarily …

**Failure scenario.** Operator follows docs/retained-v1-surface.md, registers a local_fs store under a valid grant and allowed root, calls GET /data-stores/{id}/health expecting an advisory reachability result; receives status 'unsupported' with a 'temporarily unavailable' message forever, and cannot distinguish a misconfigured root from an unimplemented feature.

**Suggested fix.** Either (a) add StoreKind.LOCAL_FS to the health-eligible kinds now that grant revalidation exists (the helper already retains the operator root and store root as nested boundaries), or (b) make the docs consistent: mark local health as deferred in docs/retained-v1-surface.md and data-store-registry-design.md, return a distinct static detail ('Local store health is not supported in this build.') instead of 'temporarily unavailable', and remove or …

**Verification.** verify=confirmed(medium)

#### M20. Git cache root/quota bypass Settings, are undocumented, and an invalid LAB_TRACKER_GIT_CACHE_MAX_BYTES silently disables the quota

`src/lab_tracker/artifact_resolution.py:3237` · error-handling · area: artifact-resolution

registry_from_env reads LAB_TRACKER_GIT_CACHE_ROOT and LAB_TRACKER_GIT_CACHE_MAX_BYTES straight from os.environ, unlike every other resolver setting which the runtime passes from the validated Settings object (which also loads a .env file). Neither variable exists in config.py nor in docs/configuration.md, so a value placed in .env is silently ignored. Worse, the quota is parsed with the lenient _env_positive_int helper, which returns the default (0 -> no quota) for any non-integer, zero or negative value, while its sibling _strict_env_positive_int (used for the recovery limits) raises. This contradicts the project's fail-loud/no-silent-fallback and config-driven standards and leaves a stale duplicate parsing helper in the module.

**Evidence.** artifact_resolution.py:3236-3237: git_cache_root = os.environ.get(LAB_TRACKER_GIT_CACHE_ROOT_ENV) or None git_max_cache_bytes = _env_positive_int(os.environ.get(LAB_TRACKER_GIT_CACHE_MAX_BYTES_ENV), 0) artifact_resolution.py:3095-3102 (_env_positive_int): `except ValueError: return default` / `return parsed if parsed > 0 else default` Reproduced: _env_positive_int('abc', 0) -> 0 _env_positive_int('-5', 0) -> 0 _strict_env_positive_int('abc', 1, variable='X') -> ValueError('X must be a positive integer.') grep 'GIT_CACHE|git_cache' src/lab_tracker/config.py docs/*.md README.md -> no matches.

**Failure scenario.** Operator sets LAB_TRACKER_GIT_CACHE_MAX_BYTES=2GB (or 2_000_000_000) in .env or the environment expecting a bounded on-disk cache. Startup succeeds without warning, git_max_cache_bytes becomes None, _enforce_cache_quota returns immediately, and the per-remote fetch caches grow without bound.

**Suggested fix.** Add git_cache_root / git_cache_max_bytes fields to Settings with validators, pass them from runtime.py into registry_from_env/default_registry like the other resolver settings, replace _env_positive_int with _strict_env_positive_int (raise on invalid input), and document both variables in docs/configuration.md.

**Verification.** verify=confirmed(medium)

#### M21. next_questions treats rejected/proposed claims as answering a question, hiding questions that still need work

`src/lab_tracker/assistant_next_questions.py:196` · correctness · area: decision-context

`_answered_question_ids` collects `answers_question_ids` from every claim regardless of claim status. A question whose only claim was `rejected` (or is still `proposed`/`testing`) is removed from the open-question set and never ranked, and the tool then reports "No active or staged unanswered questions are visible" if it was the only candidate. Reproduced with a pure-function scratch test: one planned goal, one active question, one rejected claim answering it -> payload['data'] == [] with empty_reason 'No active or staged unanswered questions are visible for the active goals.' The MCP test (tests/test_mcp_server.py:1818) only exercises an empty /claims list, so this is untested.

**Evidence.** assistant_next_questions.py:196-201: `for claim in claims: for value in claim.get('answers_question_ids') or []: answered.add(str(value))` -- no status check. :53-58 filters open_questions by `_record_id(question, 'question_id') not in answered_question_ids`. Scratch output: `NEXT QUESTIONS: {'limit': 0, 'total_candidates': 0, 'empty_reason': 'No active or staged unanswered questions are visible for the active goals.', ...}`

**Failure scenario.** Question Q is active and linked to an in-progress goal; the one claim answering Q was reviewed and set to `rejected`. lab_tracker_next_questions omits Q entirely and tells the agent there is nothing to advance, even though Q is exactly the thread that needs a new attempt.

**Suggested fix.** Only count claims whose status is `supported` (or at least exclude `rejected`) when building answered_question_ids; consider also fetching claims with a status filter in the MCP/lt clients so the 200-row cap is spent on relevant rows.

**Verification.** verify=confirmed(medium)

#### M22. Bounded subprocess cleanup depends on PID 1 reaping orphans promptly; the shipped image execs uvicorn as PID 1 with no init, so a timed-out command reports 'Subprocess cleanup failed' and leaks a zombie

`src/lab_tracker/bounded_subprocess.py:340` · correctness · area: subprocess-platform

_stop_once requires the process group to be empty within terminate_grace (0.25s) + kill_grace (1.0s). A killed grandchild that was reparented to PID 1 stays a zombie in the group until PID 1 waits for it; killpg(pgid, 0) keeps succeeding, the bound expires, and ProcessCleanupError replaces the ProcessDeadlineExceeded the caller expected. deploy/docker-entrypoint.sh `exec "$@"` makes uvicorn PID 1 and neither compose file nor render.yaml sets `init: true`; a Python server never reaps unrelated children, so in the container the zombie is permanent.

**Evidence.** Sandbox (PID 1 reaps every ~1.85s): tests/test_bounded_subprocess.py::test_leader_exit_with_inherited_pipe_is_still_bounded_by_deadline fails deterministically. Scratch subreaper.py (prctl PR_SET_CHILD_SUBREAPER, never wait): `run raised ProcessCleanupError after 1.50s`, grandchild state `Z (zombie)`.

**Failure scenario.** Docker deployment; a registered Git store fetch exceeds its deadline; the resolver reports an opaque cleanup failure instead of a deadline, and each occurrence leaves a zombie process in the container.
