**Suggested fix.** Add `init: true` to the compose services (or tini in the Dockerfile) and document it; additionally treat a group that contains only zombies as terminated (e.g. check /proc/<pid>/stat state or rely on waitpid of the reaped leader plus a bounded grace) so the invariant does not depend on the host init.

**Verification.** lead=confirmed(medium)

#### M23. ORM metadata and migrated schema have diverged: 25 columns NOT NULL in db_models but nullable in the database, 7 indexes present only in the database

`src/lab_tracker/db_models.py:414` · migration-drift · area: db-models-migrations

SQLAlchemy 2 infers nullable=False from `Mapped[T]` (non-Optional) annotations. The migrations that added these columns declared them nullable (or omitted nullable) and were never tightened, so the model promises a constraint the database does not enforce. Affected: datasets.manifest_files/manifest_external_artifacts/manifest_metadata/manifest_nwb_metadata/manifest_bids_metadata/manifest_note_ids, analyses.external_artifacts, claims.external_citations, claim_edges.created_at, data_stores.capabilities/updated_at, entity_versions.snapshot/created_at, exploration_nodes.alternatives_considered/evidence_refs, graph_draft_batch_runs.source_note_ids, group_memberships.created_at/updated_at, notes.metadata, project_groups.description/created_at/updated_at, supervision_edges.created_at/updated_at. Indexes created by migrations but absent from the model (so any metadata-driven schema, e.g. tests using Base.metadata.create_all, differs from production): ix_acquisition_outputs_session_id, ix_dataset_files_dataset_id, ix_device_enrollments_user_id, ix_device_tokens_user_id, …

**Evidence.** alembic.autogenerate.compare_metadata against a freshly migrated SQLite DB (scratchpad/q1/drift.py) reports 73 diffs, including e.g. ('modify_nullable', None, 'datasets', 'manifest_files', {...}, True, False) and ('remove_index', Index('ix_dataset_files_dataset_id', ...)). tests/test_db_wiring.py, tests/test_sqlalchemy_repository.py and 8 other test files build schema with create_all and therefore run against a stricter schema than production.

**Failure scenario.** A repository or import path (MCP write tool, backup restore, migration backfill) stores NULL in datasets.manifest_files; production accepts it, the mapper silently converts it to [], and a create_all-based test for the same path would instead raise IntegrityError, so the divergence is never caught and the manifest's provenance is quietly represented as 'no files'.

**Suggested fix.** Add a NOT NULL tightening migration (backfill '[]'/'{}'/''/CURRENT_TIMESTAMP as 0039 did) for the listed columns, add the seven indexes to db_models (or drop them in a migration if unwanted), and add a test that runs compare_metadata against the migrated schema and asserts an empty diff (with an explicit allow-list for intentional server_default differences).

**Verification.** verify=confirmed(medium)

#### M24. Decision context resolves the project through a 500-project oldest-first window, producing false anchor_not_found and dropped search matches

`src/lab_tracker/decision_context_use_case.py:198` · correctness · area: decision-context

`build_decision_context` loads `reader.list_projects(limit=CONTEXT_LOOKUP_LIMIT)` (500, ordered created_at ASC by SQLAlchemyProjectRepository.query) and later requires the resolved project to be in that dict. When the project is inferred from an entity anchor (question/dataset/analysis/claim/visualization) rather than passed explicitly, `reader.get_project` is never called for it, so a project newer than the first 500 visible projects yields `anchor_not_found: Project ... was not found` even though the anchor was found and readable. In the no-anchor path `_project_ids_with_search_matches` intersects with the same window, so matches in newer projects are silently discarded and the `ambiguous_project` candidate list omits them. Nothing reports that the project window was truncated (the `total` from list_projects is ignored). Reproduced with CONTEXT_LOOKUP_LIMIT monkeypatched to 1 (scratch test): anchor-only call returned {'code': 'anchor_not_found', 'message': "Project '422fa...' was not found."}; search call returned ambiguous_project listing only 'Older project'. The threshold in …

**Evidence.** decision_context_use_case.py:61-63 `projects_payload = reader.list_projects(limit=CONTEXT_LOOKUP_LIMIT)` ... `projects_by_id = project_lookup(projects)`; :66-69 get_project only when `resolved_project_id` was passed in; :169-170 `resolved_project_id = next(iter(anchor_project_ids))`; :198-204 `project = projects_by_id.get(resolved_project_id); if project is None: return decision_error('anchor_not_found', f'Project {resolved_project_id!r} was not found.' ...)`. core.py:104 `stmt.order_by(ProjectModel.created_at, ProjectModel.project_id)`. Scratch run output: `ANCHOR-ONLY PAYLOAD: {'code': 'anchor_not_found', ...}` and `SEARCH PAYLOAD: {'code': 'ambiguous_project', 'candidate_projects': …

**Failure scenario.** Hosted admin with 600 projects calls decision context with question_id belonging to project #550 (created most recently) and no project_id. The question is fetched successfully, its project_id is used as the scope, but projects_by_id lacks it, so the tool returns anchor_not_found for a project that exists and is readable.

**Suggested fix.** After resolving `resolved_project_id` from anchors, fetch it via `reader.get_project(resolved_project_id)` (as is already done for an explicit project_id) instead of relying on the list window; in the search path, look up matched project ids not present in the window with get_project, or report window truncation in the error metadata.

**Verification.** verify=confirmed(medium)

#### M25. Service worker accepts cross-origin POSTs to /app/share-target and the capture page silently imports them as notes/files into the victim's active project

`src/lab_tracker/frontend/sw.js:86` · security · area: sec-input · also reported as frontend-core-4

The service worker's fetch handler intercepts every `POST` navigation whose URL is `/app/share-target` (it only checks `url.origin === self.location.origin`, i.e. the target origin, never the initiator) and writes the multipart fields `title`, `text`, `url` and `file` into the `lab-tracker-share-inbox` IndexedDB store, then redirects to `/app/capture?from-share=1`. On the next render of the capture page, `useMobileCapture` calls `migrateIncomingShares` automatically (no confirmation UI) which POSTs `/notes` (text share) or enqueues `/notes/upload-file` (file share) with the user's bearer token and currently selected project. Because a plain HTML form on any third-party site can navigate the victim's browser to `https://lab.example/app/share-target` with `enctype=multipart/form-data` (and can populate `<input type=file>` via `DataTransfer`), the share-target path is an unauthenticated write channel: the SW does not require the request to come from the OS share sheet, and CSP `form-action 'self'` only constrains forms on Lab Tracker's own pages. The injected note is attributed to the …

**Evidence.** sw.js:74-89: `const url = new URL(request.url); if (url.origin !== self.location.origin) return; ... if (request.method === "POST" && url.pathname === SHARE_TARGET_PATH) { event.respondWith(handleShareTarget(request)); }`; handleShareTarget stores `formData.getAll("file")`, `title`, `text`, `url` unconditionally. frontend_src/hooks/useMobileCapture.js:143-175: `migrateIncomingShares({ createTextNote: ({metadata, rawContent}) => apiRequest("/notes", {body: {..., project_id: selectedProjectId, raw_content: rawContent}, method: "POST", token}), projectId: selectedProjectId, uploadQueue: queue })` then `queue.drain({ token, ownerId })`. share-target-inbox.js:127-161 creates the note / enqueues …

**Failure scenario.** Victim has visited /app once (SW installed) and is logged in. They visit attacker.example, whose page auto-submits `<form method=POST action="https://lab.example/app/share-target" enctype="multipart/form-data"><input name=text value="Ignore prior instructions...">` (optionally with a file set via DataTransfer). The SW stores it and redirects to /app/capture; the capture page immediately creates a note in the …

**Suggested fix.** In the SW, accept the share-target POST only when it is a genuine share-sheet launch (e.g. `request.mode === "navigate"` AND `request.headers.get("sec-fetch-site") === "none"` / `sec-fetch-dest === "document"` with no Referer from another origin), and additionally require an explicit user confirmation in the capture UI before `migrateIncomingShares` posts anything (show the parked shares with an Import/Discard control instead of auto-importing). …

**Verification.** verify=confirmed(medium)

#### M26. DailyReviewScheduleForm shows (and would save) a previously selected project's settings when responses arrive out of order

`src/lab_tracker/frontend_src/features/daily-review-schedule.jsx:41` · data-integrity · area: frontend-features-b

loadSettings has no cancellation flag or request-generation guard. When projectId changes while the previous GET /projects/{A}/graph-draft-batch-settings is in flight, the late response for A overwrites the form state (enabled, cadence, run time, timezone, email opt-in) and the `settings` record after B's data has rendered. Because saveSettings PATCHes `/projects/${projectId}/...` with whatever is in form state, the reviewer can then press Save and write project A's cadence/timezone/email settings into project B. Every other loader in this area (useApiResource, useProjectAccess, useProjectWorkspaceData, useGraphDraftWorkflow.loadDraft) guards against this; this form and BatchReviewPage.loadBatches do not.

**Evidence.** features/daily-review-schedule.jsx:41-63 — `const loadSettings = useCallback(async () => { ... const nextSettings = await apiRequest(`/projects/${projectId}/graph-draft-batch-settings`, { token }); setSettings(nextSettings); setEnabled(...); setCadenceMinutes(...); ... }, [projectId, setFlash, token]);` with no `canceled`/generation check. Scratch vitest (schedule-race.scratch.test.jsx): rendered with projectId="A", rerendered with projectId="B", resolved B (cadence 720/UTC) then A (cadence 10080/Asia/Tokyo). Result: `expect(getByLabelText("Cadence")).toHaveValue("10080")` and Time zone = "Asia/Tokyo" passed — output: "RACE REPRODUCED: form for project B now shows project A's …

**Failure scenario.** Reviewer on /app/batches switches the project selector from A to B while A's settings request is still pending (slow network, or B cached). A's response lands last; the cadence form now shows A's values under B's name. Clicking "Save cadence" PATCHes project B's personal schedule with A's cadence, run time, timezone and email opt-in.

**Suggested fix.** Add a `let canceled = false` / generation ref in loadSettings (as useApiResource does) and ignore responses whose projectId no longer matches; alternatively load settings through useApiResource and derive form state from it. Apply the same guard to BatchReviewPage.loadBatches.

**Verification.** verify=confirmed(medium)

#### M27. Note detail PATCH replaces metadata with a stale snapshot and wipes transcript provenance written by /transcript

`src/lab_tracker/frontend_src/features/notes.jsx:235` · data-integrity · area: frontend-features-a

`saveTranscript` builds `metadata = {...(note.metadata || {}), transcript_status, transcript_edited_at}` from the `note` returned by `useApiResource` at load time and PATCHes it. `handleTranscribeVoiceNote` (POST `/notes/{id}/transcript`) updates only `transcriptText`, never `note` (the hook's `setData` is unused), so the component's `note.metadata` predates the transcription. The backend transcription path merges provider provenance into metadata (`transcript_provider`, `transcript_model`, `transcript_generated_at`, `transcript_source_storage_id`; note_service.py:682-694, applied with `metadata.update(...)` in sqlalchemy_repository_parts/notes.py:276-279), but `update_note` REPLACES the bag: `note.metadata = normalize_note_metadata(metadata)` (note_service.py:802-805). So any subsequent "Save transcript" — or "Draft graph update", which auto-runs `saveTranscript({silent:true})` whenever `transcriptText !== note.transcribed_text` (always true after a transcribe, since `note.transcribed_text` is stale) — silently deletes the provider provenance. The same happens when operator-enabled …

**Evidence.** Scratch vitest: load voice note with metadata {capture_source, capture_kind, transcript_status:'pending'}, click 'Transcribe voice' (mock returns metadata with transcript_provider/model/generated_at/source_storage_id), click 'Save transcript' → `PATCH metadata after transcribe: {"capture_source":"mobile_capture","capture_kind":"voice","transcript_status":"ready","transcript_edited_at":"..."}` — provider keys absent. notes.jsx:292-296 `if (isAudio && transcriptText !== (note.transcribed_text || "")) { const updated = await saveTranscript({ silent: true });`.

**Failure scenario.** Reviewer opens a voice capture, clicks Transcribe voice, then Draft graph update (documented voice→transcript→draft workflow). The silent PATCH sends the pre-transcription metadata; the note loses transcript_provider/model/generated_at, so the record no longer says the text came from a provider model rather than a human.

**Suggested fix.** After `/transcript` succeeds, call the resource hook's `setData(updated)` so `note` reflects the server state; and either PATCH only the keys being changed (have the API merge for `metadata`) or build the PATCH bag from the freshest server copy (`updated.metadata`). Also refresh `note` after `saveTranscript` returns.

**Verification.** verify=confirmed(medium)

#### M28. Mobile capture has no in-flight guard: a double tap on Save capture creates duplicate notes

`src/lab_tracker/frontend_src/hooks/useMobileCapture.js:396` · data-integrity · area: frontend-features-a

`uploadCapture()` reads `uploadedNoteId`/`uploadedVoiceNoteId` from the render closure and only sets them after the request resolves, and nothing disables the Send / "Save for later" buttons while an upload is in flight. `setBusy(true)` is called but `busy` is never passed to `MobileCaptureCard` (app-shell.jsx:389-403), and `CaptureComposer` only disables on `!canWrite || !readyToCapture`. A second tap while the first request is pending therefore runs the whole workflow again with empty ids. The server's `client_capture_id` idempotency cannot help: `uploadOrQueueRawFile` mints a fresh `newCaptureId()` per call (shared/capture-upload.js:151) and `createTextCapture` sends no `client_capture_id` at all (capture-upload.js:183-194). On a phone with a slow uplink (multi-MB photo, voice file) a double tap is the normal failure mode. By contrast `NotePanel` disables its submit buttons on `busy` (notes.jsx:81,119).

**Evidence.** useMobileCapture.js:411-415 `let noteId = uploadedNoteId; let voiceNoteId = uploadedVoiceNoteId;` ... `if (needsText() && !noteId && !queuedOffline) { const textCapture = await createTextCapture(...)`; CaptureComposer.jsx:230 `disabled={!canWrite || !readyToCapture}` (no busy). Scratch vitest (MobileCaptureCard, slow POST /notes mock, click Save capture twice): `POST /notes count after double tap: 2` — test passed asserting two note creations; the button was still enabled after the first click.

**Failure scenario.** User types a note (or attaches a photo) on the phone, taps Send, sees no immediate feedback and taps again → two staged notes (or two raw-asset notes with different client_capture_ids) are created for one capture; both land in the pending-review queue and project counts.

**Suggested fix.** Hold an `inFlightRef`/`submitting` state in `useMobileCapture`, return early when set, and pass it to `CaptureComposer` to disable both action buttons; additionally mint one `client_capture_id` per composer session (reset in `clearUploadProgress`) and pass it to both `uploadOrQueueRawFile` and `createTextCapture` so the server idempotency key actually dedupes retries.

**Verification.** verify=confirmed(medium)

#### M29. Last-used project restore is dead inside the real App: the hook clears the stored selection on mount before auth is checked

`src/lab_tracker/frontend_src/hooks/useProjectWorkspaceData.js:167` · correctness · area: frontend-core

useProjectWorkspaceData seeds `selectedProjectId` from localStorage (`readStoredProjectId()`, line 40) and its tests assert the stored project is restored. But the effect at lines 161-171 runs `clearProjectState()` (which sets selectedProjectId to "") whenever `enabled` is false. In app-shell.jsx the hook is mounted with `enabled: apiEnabled`, and apiEnabled is false on the first render because `auth.authChecked` starts false. So on every real boot the restored id is wiped before /auth/me resolves; when `enabled` flips to true, refreshProjects sees `current === ""` and always falls back to `nextProjects[0]`. The hook's unit tests pass only because they omit `enabled` (defaults to true from the first render).

**Evidence.** useProjectWorkspaceData.js:40: useState(() => readStoredProjectId()) useProjectWorkspaceData.js:161-171: useEffect(() => { if (!enabled) { ... setProjects([]); setProjectsLoaded(false); clearProjectState(); return; } app-shell.jsx:72,89-91: apiEnabled = auth.authChecked && (...); useProjectWorkspaceData({ enabled: apiEnabled, ... }) Scratch App-level vitest (auth disabled, projects [project-1, project-2], localStorage last-used = project-2): "ACTIVE PROJECT AFTER BOOT: project-1" -> AssertionError: expected 'project-1' to be 'project-2'

**Failure scenario.** A user working in project B closes the tab; on reopening the app, project A (first in the list) is selected, and every project-scoped surface (Capture, Batches, Home) opens on the wrong project. On the capture page this means a phone capture lands in the first project unless the user notices and re-selects.

**Suggested fix.** Do not clear the stored selection while auth is merely unresolved: distinguish "not yet enabled" (leave selectedProjectId untouched, just skip fetching) from "signed out" (clear). E.g. track a ref `hasBeenEnabled` and only call clearProjectState() when enabled transitions true->false, or move the boot-time clearing into useAuthSession's clearSession path. Add an App-level test that seeds last-used-project-id and asserts the Active project select …

**Verification.** verify=confirmed(medium)

#### M30. 401 from a revoked paired-device token ("Invalid device token.") does not match the auth-rejection message pattern, so the phone session is never cleared mid-session

`src/lab_tracker/frontend_src/shared/api.js:87` · correctness · area: frontend-core

throwApiError only dispatches AUTH_REJECTED_EVENT when the 401 body message matches AUTH_REJECTION_MESSAGE_PATTERN (line 16-17). The middleware's device-principal failures use the message "Invalid device token." (app_parts/middleware.py:170,180) and PATs use "Invalid personal access token."; neither contains the literal substring "invalid token", so the regex fails. Paired phones store the device secret as their browser session token (features/enroll.jsx:61 persistTokenForReload(payload.secret)). After an admin revokes the device from the Devices page, every request from the phone fails with a flashed "Invalid device token." but useAuthSession never runs clearSession(); the revoked credential stays in localStorage and in memory, the app keeps rendering the authenticated shell, and only a full reload (boot path at useAuthSession.js:205 which ignores the message) signs the device out.

**Evidence.** api.js:16-17: /auth(entication|orization)? required|authorization header|credential|invalid token|missing authorization|session|token (has )?expired|unrecognized token/i api.js:87: if (shouldNotify && response.status === 401 && isAuthRejectedMessage(error.message)) { notifyAuthRejected(...) } middleware.py:170: raise AuthError("Invalid device token.") node regex check: "Invalid device token." false; "Invalid personal access token." false; "Invalid token." true Scratch reproduction through apiFetch with mocked 401 responses: auth-rejected events dispatched only for ["Invalid token."].

**Failure scenario.** Lab admin revokes a lost phone's device token. The phone (still open on /app/capture) keeps a live-looking session: pending-notes list errors, capture attempts flash "Invalid device token.", no sign-out, no login form, and the revoked secret remains persisted until the user manually reloads.

**Suggested fix.** Stop keying on message text: treat any 401 carrying the API error code `auth_error` (ErrorEnvelope.error.code) as an auth rejection, or at minimum extend the pattern to `invalid (device |personal access )?token`. Add a test with the exact middleware messages.

**Verification.** verify=confirmed(medium)

#### M31. Anthropic client hard-caps output at 4096 tokens and ignores stop_reason, so large batch narratives truncate into 'malformed JSON' failures

`src/lab_tracker/graph_drafting.py:799` · correctness · area: graph-drafting

`AnthropicGraphDraftClient._messages_graph_patch` sends `max_tokens: 4096` for every call, including batch drafting, whose `_batch_instructions()` demands a multi-paragraph day narrative plus one operation per finding across up to 100 notes (each operation carries payload_json, rationale and source_refs). The OpenAI and Google paths set no output cap. `_anthropic_output_text` never inspects `stop_reason`, so a `max_tokens` stop yields a truncated JSON string, `_parse_graph_patch_text` raises 'Anthropic returned malformed graph patch JSON.', the generation retries three times with the same cap, and the batch run ends FAILED with an error message that does not mention truncation.

**Evidence.** graph_drafting.py L797-804: `json={"model": self.model, "max_tokens": 4096, "system": ..., "messages": ...}`; L1595-1602 `_anthropic_output_text` returns the first text block without checking `payload.get('stop_reason')`; batch instructions L1153-1177 require a multi-paragraph narrative before operations; BATCH_NOTE_LIMIT = 100 (graph_draft_batch_policy.py L356).

**Failure scenario.** LAB_TRACKER_GRAPH_DRAFT_PROVIDER=anthropic; a day with ~40 staged notes -> the model's narrative plus ~30 operations exceed 4096 output tokens -> response cut mid-JSON -> 3 identical retries -> run FAILED 'Anthropic returned malformed graph patch JSON.' with no hint that the cap was hit.

**Suggested fix.** Raise `max_tokens` for batch/analysis calls (make it a Settings field, e.g. `anthropic_max_output_tokens`), and fail loudly when `stop_reason == 'max_tokens'` with a specific error so retries/operators can act (or retry with a larger budget).

**Verification.** verify=confirmed(medium)

#### M32. Agentic provider is accepted by config but breaks every note-scoped and inline drafting path at first use instead of at startup

`src/lab_tracker/graph_drafting.py:1140` · error-handling · area: graph-drafting

`make_graph_draft_client` returns `AgenticGraphDraftClient` for `graph_draft_provider=agentic` regardless of `graph_draft_background_enabled`. That client raises `GraphDraftingError` from `draft_from_note`/`draft_from_analysis_evidence`, and `_ensure_draft_client_allowed_here` rejects it for any non-SYSTEM actor, so with the background worker disabled every POST /notes/{id}/graph-drafts, /analysis-graph-drafts, revise, run-now and run-due request goes through three retry attempts and persists a FAILED change set / FAILED batch run. Even with the worker enabled, note-scoped drafting is permanently broken for this provider. Nothing in `Settings` validation (`config.py` has no reference to 'agentic') or app startup rejects the combination, contrary to the module's own 'fails fast at app startup' docstring and the project's fail-loud standard.

**Evidence.** graph_drafting.py L1139-1140 `if provider in {'agentic', ...}: return AgenticGraphDraftClient.from_settings(settings)`; L1060-1084 `draft_from_note`/`draft_from_analysis_evidence` unconditionally raise; graph_draft_generation.py L638-650 `_ensure_draft_client_allowed_here` raises unless `actor.is_system`; `grep -n agentic src/lab_tracker/config.py src/lab_tracker/app_parts/*.py` returns nothing.

**Failure scenario.** Operator sets LAB_TRACKER_GRAPH_DRAFT_PROVIDER=agentic without LAB_TRACKER_GRAPH_DRAFT_BACKGROUND_ENABLED: the app starts cleanly, and every 'Draft graph update' click and 'Run now' produces a FAILED record with 'only runs inside the background worker' / 'only supported for background batch drafts'.

**Suggested fix.** Validate in `Settings` (or app startup) that `graph_draft_provider=agentic` requires `graph_draft_background_enabled` and document that note-scoped drafting falls back to the wrapped single-shot client (or actually delegate `draft_from_note`/`draft_from_analysis_evidence` to the base client, which is read-only anyway).

**Verification.** impact-single=confirmed(medium)

#### M33. MCP next_questions silently truncates goals, questions, and claims at the oldest 200 rows per list with no truncation signal

`src/lab_tracker/mcp_api_client.py:690` · error-handling · area: decision-context

`LabTrackerAPIClient.next_questions` fetches `list_goals(..., limit=200)`, `list_questions(..., status=..., limit=200)` and `list_claims(project_id, limit=200)` with no offset paging and no recent_first, then hands the partial lists to `build_next_questions_payload`. The API caps limit at 200 (routes/shared.py:220), and the list endpoints order oldest-first by default, so on a project with more than 200 claims the newest claims (the ones most likely to answer open questions) are never seen and questions they answer are ranked as unanswered; goals or questions beyond 200 are simply dropped. The returned payload's meta carries `total_candidates` but nothing about the truncated inputs, contradicting the fail-loud standard and the decision-context tooling's own truncation contract. The lt client version uses `_list_all` and does page, so the two clients diverge.

**Evidence.** mcp_api_client.py:667-692: `payload = self.list_goals(project_id=project_id, status=status, limit=200)` ... `self.list_questions(project_id=lookup_project_id, status=status, limit=200)` ... `claims.extend(_payload_items(self.list_claims(project_id=lookup_project_id, limit=200)))`; list_claims (487-510) passes only limit/offset, no recent_first. routes/shared.py:221 `if limit < 1 or limit > 200: raise ValidationError`. lab_tracker_client/client.py:828-839 uses `self.list_goals(...)`/`_list_all` paging instead.

**Failure scenario.** A mature project has 250 claims; claim #240 (newest) answers question Q. MCP next_questions loads claims 1-200, does not see #240, and ranks Q as the top unanswered question with next_action pointing the agent to re-answer it.

**Suggested fix.** Page through the list endpoints (as the lt client does) or at minimum pass `recent_first=true`, compare `meta.total` to the rows fetched, and add a `truncated`/`inputs_truncated` flag to the payload meta when any list was cut.

**Verification.** verify=confirmed(medium)

#### M34. FastMCP auto-enables DNS-rebinding Host validation for the documented default bind 127.0.0.1, so the hosted endpoint answers 421 behind the Caddy proxy (reproduced)

`src/lab_tracker/mcp_server.py:172` · correctness · area: mcp

mcp 1.27's `FastMCP.__init__` sets `transport_security = TransportSecuritySettings(enable_dns_rebinding_protection=True, allowed_hosts=["127.0.0.1:*","localhost:*","[::1]:*"], allowed_origins=["http://127.0.0.1:*", ...])` whenever `host in ("127.0.0.1","localhost","::1")` and no explicit settings are passed. `build_server` passes `host=settings.host` without `transport_security`. docs/configuration.md:856-858 and MCPServerRuntimeSettings default `LAB_TRACKER_MCP_HOST=127.0.0.1` ('loopback, proxy owns TLS' per the design doc), and deploy/mcp/Caddyfile reverse-proxies to 127.0.0.1:9000 while (Caddy default) preserving the client Host `mcp.lab.internal`. Every proxied request is therefore rejected with `421 Invalid Host header` before the bearer middleware's downstream even sees a JSON-RPC message; a client that sends the Caddyfile-allowed `Origin: https://github.com` would additionally get 403 from FastMCP's origin allowlist. The docker-compose path works only because it binds 0.0.0.0, which disables the protection entirely. tests/test_mcp_server.py:328-360 only exercises Host …

**Evidence.** mcp_server.py:171-183: `if settings.transport == "streamable-http": kwargs = {"host": settings.host, "port": settings.port, "streamable_http_path": settings.path, "json_response": True, "stateless_http": True}` (no transport_security). Installed mcp 1.27.2 FastMCP.__init__: `if transport_security is None and host in ("127.0.0.1", "localhost", "::1"): transport_security = TransportSecuritySettings(enable_dns_rebinding_protection=True, allowed_hosts=["127.0.0.1:*", "localhost:*", "[::1]:*"], ...)`. Reproduction (scratch, ASGITransport through MCPInboundBearerAuthMiddleware with a valid bearer, POST initialize): base_url http://127.0.0.1:9000 -> `200 …

**Failure scenario.** An operator runs `lt-mcp` as a bare process (systemd, Windows service, or a Tailscale `serve` setup) with the documented defaults `LAB_TRACKER_MCP_TRANSPORT=streamable-http`, `LAB_TRACKER_MCP_HOST=127.0.0.1`, puts the checked-in Caddyfile in front, and every Copilot/Cursor connection fails with HTTP 421 despite a correct inbound token; the 421 is emitted by FastMCP after the auth middleware, so the operator's own …

**Suggested fix.** Pass an explicit `transport_security=TransportSecuritySettings(enable_dns_rebinding_protection=False)` for the hosted transport (the inbound bearer already defeats rebinding, and the proxy owns Host/Origin policy), or expose `LAB_TRACKER_MCP_ALLOWED_HOSTS`/`ALLOWED_ORIGINS` env settings and forward them; either way add a test that initializes through the middleware with a non-loopback Host header, and either strip `Origin`/rewrite `Host` in the …

**Verification.** verify=confirmed(medium)

#### M35. Hosted streamable-http server registers all write tools and local-file-reading tools; nothing verifies the server-held LPAT is read-only, and upload_visualization_file leaks a host file-existence oracle

`src/lab_tracker/mcp_server.py:185` · security · area: mcp · also reported as sec-input-6

docs/copilot-mcp-support-design.md ('All remote/hosted surfaces are read-only', guardrail table row 'Hosted (Mode 2) Writes: no, enforced by read-only lpat_'), docs/lab-tracker-mcp-skills.md:44-64 and docs/deployment-options.md:54-66 promise a read-only hosted endpoint. In code, `build_server()` calls `register_write_tools` unconditionally for the streamable-http transport, and `main()`'s only target check (`_ensure_mcp_target_safe`) verifies `auth.enabled`, never that `LAB_TRACKER_MCP_API_KEY` is a `read_only=True` token (compose merely names the variable LT_MCP_READONLY_TOKEN). If an operator pastes a write-enabled LPAT (the default `lt setup connect --save-token` flow and `POST /auth/tokens` both allow `read_only=false`), the shared inbound bearer becomes a remote write credential for 14 tools and a remote channel for `lab_tracker_upload_visualization_file(file_path=...)` / `record_evidence_bundle(visualization={upload_file: true, ...})`, which read arbitrary files from the MCP container/host and push them into managed storage where any project member can download them. Even with …

**Evidence.** mcp_server.py:184-186: `register_read_tools(runtime_server); register_write_tools(runtime_server); register_resources(runtime_server)` with no transport branch. mcp_server.py:237-260 `_ensure_mcp_target_safe` only inspects `auth.get("enabled")`. mcp_api_client.py:1093-1095: `path = Path(file_path).expanduser(); if not path.is_file(): raise LabTrackerAPIError(f"Visualization file does not exist: {file_path}")`. Scratch run with a MockTransport simulating a read-only LPAT (403): `/etc/passwd -> LabTrackerAPIAuthError Not permitted for this token.`; `/etc/does-not-exist -> LabTrackerAPIError Visualization file does not exist: /etc/does-not-exist`; `/proc/self/environ -> …

**Failure scenario.** Operator sets LT_MCP_READONLY_TOKEN to an LPAT minted with default settings that happen to be write-enabled (or reuses their personal token). A remote IDE agent (or any client on the tailnet that learns the shared inbound bearer) calls `lab_tracker_upload_visualization_file(viz_id=<any viz>, file_path="/app/data/runtime-env/auth-secret-key")` and the file becomes downloadable via `/visualizations/{id}/file`; or …

**Suggested fix.** In `build_server`, skip `register_write_tools` (and any tool that reads local files) when `settings.transport == "streamable-http"`, or add an explicit `LAB_TRACKER_MCP_ALLOW_WRITES` opt-in that fails closed by default. At startup in hosted mode, probe the token's capability (e.g. attempt a harmless write such as `POST /evidence-bundles` dry-run, or add an introspection endpoint outside `/auth`) and refuse to boot unless the API reports the …

**Verification.** verify=confirmed(medium)

#### M36. Startup open-ADMIN guard fails open: any non-auth API error (unreachable target, 404, 5xx) is swallowed silently and the server boots unguarded

`src/lab_tracker/mcp_server.py:251` · error-handling · area: mcp

`_ensure_mcp_target_safe` exists to refuse serving a non-loopback API whose `/readiness` reports `auth.enabled=false` (the 'Open-ADMIN footgun' in docs/copilot-mcp-support-design.md:311-316). The auth-failure branch at least warns to stderr (GH #79), but `except LabTrackerAPIError: return` at 251-252 covers `LabTrackerAPIUnavailableError` (connection refused/timeout/5xx) and every other status with no log line at all. In hosted deployments the MCP container is restarted independently of `app` (`restart: unless-stopped`; `depends_on: service_healthy` only orders the first start), so an MCP restart while `app` is down or returning 502 through a proxy boots the server with the guard skipped, and a later auth-disabled `app` is served without complaint. This contradicts the project's fail-loud/no-silent-fallback standard and makes the guard unreliable exactly in the outage scenarios where configuration is most likely to be changed.

**Evidence.** mcp_server.py:242-254: ``` try: payload = client.readiness() except LabTrackerAPIAuthError as exc: _warn_startup_auth_probe_failed(settings, exc) return except LabTrackerAPIError: return ``` No stderr output, no retry, no exit code on the second branch. tests/test_mcp_server.py covers only the loopback skip, the auth-disabled refusal and the auth-401 warning; there is no test for the unavailable branch.

**Failure scenario.** `docker compose restart mcp` while `app` is mid-upgrade (returning 502 from its healthcheck window): `client.readiness()` raises LabTrackerAPIUnavailableError, the guard returns silently, the hosted MCP endpoint comes up; if the operator's `app` env lost `LAB_TRACKER_AUTH_SECRET_KEY`/`LAB_TRACKER_ENVIRONMENT` in that upgrade, the endpoint now serves an auth-disabled ADMIN API to every holder of the shared inbound …

**Suggested fix.** Treat the hosted (streamable-http) case fail-closed: exit non-zero when the probe cannot confirm `auth.enabled` (let compose restart policy retry), or at minimum retry with backoff and print a loud stderr warning mirroring `_warn_startup_auth_probe_failed`; for stdio keep fail-soft but still warn. Add a test for the unavailable branch.

**Verification.** verify=confirmed(medium)

#### M37. MCP decision-context tool cannot pass created_by/since/until, so the documented progress_review briefing is unreachable from MCP

`src/lab_tracker/mcp_tools/read.py:716` · contract-drift · area: decision-context

docs/retained-v1-surface.md (lines 155-165) and docs/mcp-decision-context-tooling.md (lines 93-100) document that the decision-context endpoint takes `created_by`, `since`, `until` and that `progress_review` is scoped to a person and a time window. The HTTP route (schemas.AssistantDecisionContextRequest) accepts them and tests/test_activity_briefing.py covers the route. But `lab_tracker_get_decision_context` (mcp_tools/read.py:716) and `LabTrackerAPIClient.get_decision_context` (mcp_api_client.py:633) have no such parameters and never forward them, while the tool docstring still advertises `progress_review` as an allowed task_kind. The `lt` client has no decision-context method at all. An agent asking for a progress briefing via MCP therefore gets an unscoped project dump labelled `progress_review` with no error, and there is no MCP test for the scoped path (grep of tests/test_mcp*.py for created_by/since with decision-context returns nothing).

**Evidence.** mcp_tools/read.py:716-750 signature: `task_kind, query, project_id, question_id, dataset_id, analysis_id, claim_id, visualization_id, limit` only; docstring: "Allowed task_kind values: ... progress_review". mcp_api_client.py:633-663 json_payload has no created_by/since/until keys. schemas.py:818-830 AssistantDecisionContextRequest has `created_by: UUID | None`, `since`, `until`. docs/retained-v1-surface.md:161-164: "The decision-context endpoint exposes the same created_by/since/until filters plus a progress_review task kind, assembling a person-scoped, windowed briefing in one call."

**Failure scenario.** A PI's MCP client calls lab_tracker_get_decision_context(task_kind='progress_review', query='what did Alice do since last meeting', project_id=P). The server receives no created_by/since/until, returns the newest N notes/sessions/datasets/analyses/claims by anyone in P, and the agent presents that as Alice's progress with no indication that the filter was silently dropped.

**Suggested fix.** Add `created_by: str | None`, `since: str | None`, `until: str | None` to the MCP tool and to LabTrackerAPIClient.get_decision_context, forward them in the POST body, add an MCP test mirroring test_progress_review_briefing_is_person_and_window_scoped, and either add the same to the lt client or note in docs that only MCP/HTTP expose it.

**Verification.** verify=confirmed(medium)

#### M38. All MCP tools are synchronous and run inline on the FastMCP event loop, so one slow API call stalls every concurrent hosted client

`src/lab_tracker/mcp_tools/read.py:814` · performance · area: mcp

Every read/write tool is a plain `def` that performs blocking `httpx.Client` I/O (default 10 s timeout, and `next_questions` fans out 2 + 3×projects sequential requests). mcp 1.27's `FuncMetadata.call_fn_with_arg_validation` executes non-async tools directly (`return fn(**arguments_parsed_dict)`), not in a threadpool, and the streamable-http app runs them on uvicorn's single event loop. In stdio mode this is harmless (one client), but the hosted 'stateless_http' endpoint is explicitly meant to serve many IDE agents; a single agent calling `lab_tracker_next_questions()` across a large lab, or any request that hits the 10 s timeout against a slow Postgres, blocks initialize/list_tools/health for every other agent, including the auth middleware's 401 responses which queue behind the busy loop. The module-global cached `httpx.Client` in `_read_client()` is also shared by all requests, which is only safe because of this serialization.

**Evidence.** read.py:811-814 `for tool in READ_TOOLS: server.tool(title=title, annotations=...)(tool)` registering sync functions; read.py:81-83 `client = _read_client(); return with_next_action(call(client), hint)` (blocking httpx). Installed mcp 1.27.2 `mcp/server/fastmcp/utilities/func_metadata.py`: `if fn_is_async: return await fn(**arguments_parsed_dict) else: return fn(**arguments_parsed_dict)` (verified via inspect.getsource). mcp_api_client.py:674-692 `next_questions` issues `list_goals` per open status then, per project, 2×`list_questions` + `list_claims`, each `limit=200`.

**Failure scenario.** Two Copilot users share the hosted endpoint. User A's agent calls `lab_tracker_get_decision_context` while the API is slow (query takes 8 s); user B's agent sends `initialize` and `tools/list`, which sit unanswered for 8 s and the IDE reports the server as hung. With a handful of agents polling, the endpoint becomes effectively single-user.

**Suggested fix.** Make the registered tool functions `async def` and run the blocking client via `anyio.to_thread.run_sync` (or switch LabTrackerAPIClient to `httpx.AsyncClient`), keeping one client per task or a thread-safe pool; alternatively register thin async wrappers only for the streamable-http transport. Add a hosted-mode test that runs two concurrent tool calls against a delayed mock API and asserts the second is not serialized behind the first.

**Verification.** verify=confirmed(medium)

#### M39. Nested domain models reused inside request schemas silently drop unknown/misspelled keys (extra='ignore'), contradicting the extra='forbid' request policy

`src/lab_tracker/models.py:468` · contract-drift · area: domain-models

RequestModel sets extra='forbid' so top-level typos fail loudly, but the nested types embedded in request payloads are domain models built on _DomainModel (ConfigDict(from_attributes=True) only): EntityRef (NoteCreate.targets, ExplorationNodeCreate.target/evidence_refs), QuestionLink (DatasetUpdate.question_links), DatasetCommitManifestInput/DatasetFile (DatasetCreate/DatasetUpdate/SessionDatasetPromotionRequest.commit_manifest), ExternalArtifactReference (claims/analyses/manifests), ClaimInput and VisualizationInput (AnalysisCommitRequest). Pydantic's default extra='ignore' means unknown keys inside these objects are dropped without a 422 or an issue entry, so a misspelled optional field (e.g. `falsifcation_criteria` on a commit-analysis claim, `sizeBytes` on a manifest file) is silently lost and the record is persisted incomplete. This violates the project's 'fail loud, no silent fallbacks' standard and the intent of RequestModel.

**Evidence.** Scratch test: DatasetCreate.model_validate({..., 'commit_manifest': {'files': [{'path':'a','checksum':'b','sizeBytes':5}], 'bogus':1}}) validates; dumped manifest is {'files': [{'file_id': None, 'path': 'a', 'checksum': 'b', 'size_bytes': None}], ...} (sizeBytes and bogus gone). ClaimCreate with external_citations=[{..., 'bogus':1}] validates. NoteCreate targets=[{'entity_type':'claim','entity_id':..., 'role':'x'}] validates. AnalysisCommitRequest.claims=[{'statement':'s','confidence':5,'falsifcation_criteria':'typo'}] validates with falsification_criteria None. models.py:468-469 `class _DomainModel(BaseModel): model_config = ConfigDict(from_attributes=True)`; schemas.py:116-117 `class …

**Failure scenario.** An agent or script commits an analysis with claims whose `falsification_criteria` key is misspelled, or uploads a manifest with camelCase `sizeBytes`. The API returns 201/200, the claim is stored with no falsification criteria and the manifest file with size_bytes null, and nobody is told; the pre-registered criterion the researcher wrote is lost from the provenance record.

**Suggested fix.** Define explicit request-side wrappers (or subclass the nested models with model_config = ConfigDict(extra='forbid')) for every nested type used inside RequestModel payloads, keeping the from_attributes domain models for responses only.

**Verification.** verify=confirmed(medium)

#### M40. Claim provenance omits visualizations related to the claim unless their analysis is a supporting analysis

`src/lab_tracker/provenance.py:665` · correctness · area: evidence-provenance

context_queries.claim_provenance deliberately loads every visualization related to the claim (query_visualizations(claim_id=...)) and passes them to build_claim_provenance_document, but the builder only emits visualizations by routing them through build_analysis_provenance_document for analyses in claim.supported_by_analysis_ids, filtered by `visualization.analysis_id == analysis.analysis_id` (lines 664-669). A visualization with related_claim_ids containing the claim but generated by any other analysis (e.g. a claim supported directly by a dataset, or a figure produced by a follow-up analysis and attached to the claim via the evidence-bundle `related_claim_ids`) is silently dropped, along with its generating analysis. The fetched data is discarded and the exported claim sidecar is incomplete.

**Evidence.** provenance.py:654-669 only iterates `for analysis in analyses` and passes `visualizations=[v for v in visualizations if v.analysis_id == analysis.analysis_id]`; no other code path emits visualization nodes in this builder. Scratch test (scratchpad/p3/test_claim_viz.py): claim supported by a dataset only; Visualization(analysis_id=other_analysis, related_claim_ids=[claim]); build_claim_provenance_document(..., analyses=[], datasets=[dataset], visualizations=[viz]) -> @graph ids = [dataset, dataset/provenance/commit, agents/u, claims/<id>]; the visualization IRI is absent (assert passed). The route test test_claim_provenance_route_exports_full_ancestry only covers the analysis-supported path.

**Failure scenario.** Agent records an evidence bundle: claim supported_by_dataset_ids=[D], visualization created under analysis A2 with related_claim_ids inferred to the bundle claim. GET /claims/{id}/provenance (and the `lt export` claim sidecar) contains no lab:Visualization node and no A2 node, although /visualizations?claim_id= lists it.

**Suggested fix.** After the analysis loop, emit any visualization in `visualizations` whose IRI is not yet in `merged` via _visualization_node (with attribution/supervision), and add a summary node for its analysis if absent; or have the handler also load the analyses of related visualizations and pass them explicitly.

**Verification.** verify=confirmed(medium)

#### M41. Claim provenance document overwrites merged prov:Person nodes and drops dataset/analysis-time supervision (actedOnBehalfOf)

`src/lab_tracker/provenance.py:718` · correctness · area: evidence-provenance

build_claim_provenance_document merges the full dataset and analysis sub-documents into `merged` with setdefault (first writer wins), then unconditionally overwrites every person node with the claim-level `people` dict (lines 716-718). The claim-level people are computed only with activity_time=claim.created_at, so a dataset creator's `actedOnBehalfOf` edge that was active when the dataset was committed (and that the dataset's own /provenance document emits) is silently removed from the claim document, while the supervisor's prov:Person node from the dataset sub-document survives as an orphan. build_analysis_provenance_document accumulates relationships across activity times in one `people` dict, so the claim and record-export documents (same pattern at lines 2053-2055) are inconsistent with the analysis and dataset documents. `lt export` writes one sidecar per supported claim, so the archived claim sidecar loses supervision context that the dataset sidecar records.

**Evidence.** provenance.py:716-718: for user_id in sorted(people): person = people[user_id] merged[str(person["@id"])] = person Scratch test (scratchpad/p3/test_claim_supervision.py): dataset created 2025-06-01 by creator with supervision edge 2025-01-01..2025-12-31; claim created 2026-03-01 supported by that dataset. build_dataset_provenance_document -> creator node has actedOnBehalfOf. build_claim_provenance_document(... datasets=[dataset], supervision_edges=[edge]) -> creator node: {'@id': '.../agents/aaaa...0001', '@type': 'prov:Person', 'userId': ...} with NO actedOnBehalfOf (assert passed), while '.../agents/aaaa...0002' (the supervisor) is still present in @graph as an orphan node.

**Failure scenario.** A student commits a dataset while supervised; supervision ends (graduation/rotation); later a claim is recorded citing that dataset. GET /claims/{id}/provenance and the `lt export` claim sidecar show the dataset creator without the supervision relationship the dataset sidecar asserts, and include a dangling supervisor Person node.

**Suggested fix.** Do not overwrite merged person nodes; instead merge relationships: pass a single shared `people` dict into the dataset/analysis sub-builders (as build_analysis_provenance_document already does internally), or when finalising, union `actedOnBehalfOf` entries from the already-merged node with the claim-level ones using the same dedup key as _add_person_with_supervision. Apply the same fix to build_record_export_provenance_document.

**Verification.** verify=confirmed(medium)

#### M42. InMemoryRateLimiter never evicts expired buckets; per-token PAT keys make the dict grow without bound from invalid tokens

`src/lab_tracker/rate_limit.py:48` · performance · area: config-failure-modes

`_current_bucket` replaces an expired bucket only when the same key is seen again; nothing ever removes other keys, and `reset()` is only called on success. The PAT middleware keys buckets by `lpat:{client_host}:{sha256(token)[:24]}` (app_parts/middleware.py:348-351), so every distinct invalid `lpat_` bearer creates a permanent entry, and the limiter cannot limit a client that rotates tokens (each guess gets its own 10-attempt budget). Login/register keys (`login:{host}:{username}`, routes/auth.py:298-303) also accumulate per (IP, username) forever. This is process memory in the hosted/shared-provider deployments that sit behind a proxy where `request.client.host` is constant.

**Evidence.** Scratch repro: 50 000 `record_failure` calls with distinct keys, sleep past the 1 s window, then `check()` -> `len(rl._buckets) == 50001`. tests/test_personal_access_token_routes.py:382 only tests the max-attempts path; no test covers eviction.

**Failure scenario.** An unauthenticated client on a hosted instance streams requests with random `lpat_...` bearers (or random usernames to /auth/login); each creates a bucket that is never freed, growing the worker's RSS until it is OOM-killed, while the PAT brute-force limiter never triggers because each token has its own bucket.

**Suggested fix.** Prune expired buckets on every `_current_bucket` call (or cap the dict size with an LRU), and key the PAT limiter by client host only (optionally plus a coarse prefix) rather than by token hash. Add a test asserting expired keys are dropped.

**Verification.** verify=confirmed(medium)

#### M43. InMemoryRateLimiter buckets are never pruned and are keyed by attacker-controlled strings (unbounded usernames, per-token hashes), so it neither bounds memory nor limits PAT guessing

`src/lab_tracker/rate_limit.py:50` · performance · area: auth · also reported as sec-input-4, concurrency-perf-1

`_current_bucket` inserts a new `_Bucket` for every unseen key and only replaces (never deletes) an expired bucket when the same key is touched again; `reset` is called only on successful login / valid PAT. Login keys are `login:{host}:{username}` where `username` is `NonBlankStr` with no max_length (schemas.py:91) and register keys are per host; PAT keys are `lpat:{host}:{sha256(token)[:24]}` (middleware.py:348-351). An unauthenticated client can therefore create one permanent bucket per distinct username or per distinct guessed token. Because the PAT key includes the token itself, every new guess starts at attempts=0, so the limiter never throttles a token-guessing loop — it only throttles re-presenting the same wrong token (which is exactly what the existing test checks).

**Evidence.** `python -c` probe: 200,000 `record_failure` calls with distinct usernames from one IP -> `len(rl._buckets) == 200000`, ~54 MB (with short keys; usernames are unbounded so each key can be far larger). Scratch test test_pat_rate_limiter_never_trips_for_distinct_invalid_tokens with `max_attempts=2`: 50 requests with `lpat_guess-{i}` -> statuses {401} only, and `len(pat_rate_limiter._buckets) == 50`. Code: rate_limit.py:46-52 (no eviction), routes/auth.py:298-303 (username in key), middleware.py:348-351 (token hash in key).

**Failure scenario.** An unauthenticated attacker (or a misbehaving script) sends login attempts with random multi-KB usernames or random lpat_ tokens at a few hundred requests/second; process RSS grows without bound until the container is OOM-killed. Separately, a brute-force loop against lpat_ tokens is never rate limited by this component.

**Suggested fix.** Bound the limiter: evict expired buckets opportunistically (e.g. sweep on insert when size exceeds N) and cap total buckets (LRU). Bound username length in `AuthLoginRequest`/`AuthRegisterRequest`. Key the PAT limiter by client host only (not by token) so repeated invalid tokens from one peer are throttled together; keep a separate per-token key only if needed for the 'same token' case.

**Verification.** verify=confirmed(medium)

#### M44. Session JWTs cannot be revoked: an admin password reset or role change never terminates existing sessions, and /auth/refresh extends them indefinitely

`src/lab_tracker/routes/auth.py:227` · security · area: auth

Access tokens are stateless HS256 JWTs carrying only sub/role/iat/exp (auth.py:498-512); verification (auth.py:514-540) checks only signature and exp. There is no token version, password-hash binding, or denylist, so resetting a compromised user's password via PATCH /auth/users/{id} leaves the attacker's session valid for the remaining TTL (default 12h), and `/auth/refresh` (routes/auth.py:220-228) issues a brand-new full-TTL token from any valid token with no absolute session lifetime, so a stolen token that is refreshed at least every 12h never expires.

**Evidence.** Scratch test test_session_token_survives_admin_password_reset: after admin `PATCH /auth/users/{victim}` with a new password, the pre-existing victim JWT still gets `GET /auth/me` -> 200 and `POST /auth/refresh` -> 200 (new token minted). Code: `token = token_service.issue_access_token(user)` on refresh with no check beyond the existing token being valid; `TokenClaims` has no version/jti.

**Failure scenario.** An agent host or laptop is compromised and the session JWT leaks. The admin rotates the user's password (the only remediation offered). The attacker keeps full access and can refresh forever as long as they call /auth/refresh within 12 hours.

**Suggested fix.** Embed a per-user credential version (e.g. a `token_version` column or a hash of `password_hash`) in the JWT and reject tokens whose version does not match the live user on every request; bump it on password/role change. Give refresh an absolute maximum session age (e.g. carry the original iat and refuse refresh after N days).

**Verification.** verify=confirmed(medium)

#### M45. Authorization denials ("Project contributor access required") are raised as AuthError and mapped to HTTP 401 auth_error, which the CLI/MCP clients treat as credential rejection

`src/lab_tracker/routes/errors.py:88` · contract-drift · area: domain-models · also reported as routes-http-4

errors.py defines a single AuthError for 'Authentication or authorization failure' and routes/errors.py maps it to 401 with code 'auth_error'. ProjectAuthorizationPolicy raises AuthError for every membership/role denial (project_authorization.py:192,216,228 and the group/owner variants), so an authenticated user who lacks a role on a project gets 401 rather than 403, while the middleware already uses 403 with distinct codes for device/service forbiddance (app_parts/middleware.py:92-99) and StoreAuthorityDeniedError maps to 403. Consumers key off the status: lab_tracker_client/transport.py:166 retries any 401 by calling refresh_bearer, and client.py:362-372 raises 'LAB_TRACKER_ACCESS_TOKEN was rejected by the Lab Tracker API. Refresh the token or set LAB_TRACKER_USERNAME and LAB_TRACKER_PASSWORD' when only a token is configured, or re-logs in with username/password and replays the request when credentials exist. mcp_api_client.py implements the same single-401-retry. The frontend had to add a message regex (api.js:16-17 AUTH_REJECTION_MESSAGE_PATTERN) to avoid logging users out on …

**Evidence.** Scratch test: an authenticated EDITOR with no membership on a project POSTs /notes -> 401 {'error': {'code': 'auth_error', 'message': 'Project contributor access required.'}}. Code: errors.py:20-21 `class AuthError(LabTrackerError): "Authentication or authorization failure."`; routes/errors.py:88-96 maps AuthError to HTTP_401_UNAUTHORIZED; lab_tracker_client/transport.py:166 `if response.status_code == 401 and authenticated and retry_on_unauthorized: headers['Authorization'] = f'Bearer {self._auth.refresh_bearer(response)}'`; client.py:364-369 raises the token-rejected message when no login credentials exist.

**Failure scenario.** A lab member runs `lt` (or an MCP agent runs a write tool) with a valid personal access token against a project they only view. The server answers 401 'Project contributor access required.'; the client discards the token and reports 'LAB_TRACKER_ACCESS_TOKEN was rejected by the Lab Tracker API', sending the user to re-mint a token instead of asking for project access. With username/password configured the client …

**Suggested fix.** Split the taxonomy: keep AuthError -> 401 for authentication failures and add a ForbiddenError (or PermissionDeniedError) -> 403 'forbidden' for ProjectAuthorizationPolicy/goal-scope denials; update clients to only refresh on 401.

**Verification.** verify=confirmed(medium)

#### M46. GET /batches loads every batch change set across all projects, with operations and context packets, then filters and paginates in memory

`src/lab_tracker/routes/graph_batches.py:96` · performance · area: graph-drafting · also reported as concurrency-perf-2

`list_batches` calls `list_batch_graph_drafts(project_id=project_id, status=...)`, which goes through `GraphDraftRecords.list_graph_change_sets(limit=None, include_operations=True)`; only afterwards does it filter by status, `filter_project_scoped_items`, `mine`/`needs_commit` (each doing a membership lookup per item), and `paginate` in Python. The frontend hits this endpoint without a project filter on every app open (`PendingBatchBanner` fetches `/batches?limit=5&mine=true`) and four times per Daily Review page load. Each hydrated `GraphChangeSet` carries its full `context_packet` (batch packets budget up to 256 KB of source text plus per-project graph context) and all operations, and the response model also serializes `context_packet` for each returned item. On a multi-project or hosted instance the cost grows with total batch history, not with the page. `/graph-drafts` already solved this with `query_graph_change_sets(include_operations=False)` and SQL pagination.

**Evidence.** routes/graph_batches.py L96-99 `change_sets = api.list_batch_graph_drafts(project_id=project_id, status=effective_status)` then L100-139 in-memory filtering and `paginate(visible, limit, offset)`; graph_draft_records.py L273-283 `query_graph_change_sets(... limit=None, offset=0)` (include_operations defaults True); repository `_from_rows` loads operations for every row (graph_drafts.py L327-368); frontend batches.jsx L36 `buildApiPath('/batches', { limit: 5, mine: true })` and L143-160 with `project_id: selectedProjectId` which `buildApiPath` drops when empty.

**Failure scenario.** Lab with 10 projects and a year of daily batches (~3,600 change sets, each with a multi-hundred-KB context packet): every app open and every Daily Review refresh materializes all of them (tens to hundreds of MB) to return 5-100 rows; latency and memory grow unboundedly and other requests on a SQLite host stall behind it.

**Suggested fix.** Push status/draft_mode/project scoping into SQL via `query_graph_change_sets(project_ids=accessible_ids, draft_mode=GRAPH_BATCH, status=..., limit, offset, include_operations=False)`, resolve `mine`/assignee and owner filters as SQL predicates (review_assignee_user_id = actor, membership join), and return a summary shape without `context_packet` for list views.

**Verification.** verify=confirmed(medium)

#### M47. Quick-capture writes the upload to raw note storage before authorization and project-existence checks

`src/lab_tracker/routes/notes.py:172` · security · area: routes-http · also reported as file-storage-3

`POST /notes/quick-capture` calls `request_api.store_note_raw_asset(file.file, ...)` (a full write + fsync of up to `max_upload_bytes`, default 100 MiB, into the raw note store) before any contributor check. The contributor/existence checks only run later inside `upload_note_raw_result`, which then deletes the just-written asset when it raises. The sibling route `POST /notes/upload-file` (line 107) calls `ensure_project_contributor` before storing, and the read side has an explicit invariant that denied requests never touch storage (`test_denied_raw_note_read_never_touches_storage`). Any authenticated principal, including one with no project memberships, can therefore drive repeated full-size disk writes/fsyncs/deletes against the raw note store for projects they cannot access or that do not exist.

**Evidence.** notes.py 161-186: `actor = actor_from_request(request)` ... `asset = request_api.store_note_raw_asset(file.file, ...)` ... `result = request_api.upload_note_raw_result(project_id=project_id, raw_asset=asset, owns_raw_asset=True, ...)`; note_service.py 411 `self.authorization.require_contributor(project_id, actor=actor)` runs only inside upload_note_raw_result. Scratch test (spy on app.state.raw_note_storage.store_stream): quick-capture as non-member of hidden project -> 401 with calls=['store_stream']; quick-capture for nonexistent project -> 404 with calls=['store_stream']; /notes/upload-file as non-member -> 401 with calls=[].

**Failure scenario.** A viewer-only account (or any self-registered account) POSTs 100 MiB bodies to /notes/quick-capture with a random project_id in a loop; each request is fully written and fsynced to the note store, then unlinked, saturating disk I/O on a shared/hosted instance while every response is a 401/404.

**Suggested fix.** Call `ensure_project_contributor(request, project_id)` (as upload_note_file does) before `enforce_request_content_length_limit`/`store_note_raw_asset`, or move the store call after authorization inside the service.

**Verification.** verify=confirmed(medium)

#### M48. /usage-events/export materializes the entire usage-event table in memory with no bound

`src/lab_tracker/routes/usage_events.py:517` · performance · area: routes-http

The export route calls `query_usage_events(..., limit=None, offset=0)`, and the repository applies no cap when `limit` is None (`apply_pagination(stmt, limit=None)`), so every matching row is loaded as a `UsageEvent` model, run through `jsonable_encoder`, and joined into one Python string before a non-streaming `Response` is built. Usage events are written for every entity view, search, create, update and upload (see `record_usage_view` in every GET detail route) and are only rolled up after 365 days, so on a multi-user hosted instance this table reaches millions of rows; one admin click on export can exhaust the worker's memory and stall the event loop's threadpool for all tenants.

**Evidence.** usage_events.py 511-519: `events, _ = api_from_request(request, api).query_usage_events(..., limit=None, offset=0)`; 520: `encoded = [jsonable_encoder(event) for event in events]`; 526: `body = "\n".join(...)`. sqlalchemy_repository_parts/usage.py 166: `rows = self._session.scalars(apply_pagination(stmt, limit=limit, offset=offset))` with no ceiling for limit=None.

**Failure scenario.** Instance with ~2M usage events over a year; admin requests GET /usage-events/export?format=csv; the process allocates several GB building models + dicts + one string, gets OOM-killed or blocks for minutes, taking down the API for every other user.

**Suggested fix.** Stream the export (StreamingResponse with a generator that pages through the repository in fixed-size batches), or require/clamp a date window or row cap and return 413/422 when the selection exceeds it.

**Verification.** verify=confirmed(medium)

#### M49. Request schemas leave name/title/description/viz_type/etc. unbounded while ORM columns are String(255)/String(40)/String(1000): SQLite silently accepts, Postgres will raise DataError -> HTTP 500

`src/lab_tracker/schemas.py:1034` · contract-drift · area: domain-models

Several request fields have no max_length but map to length-limited VARCHAR columns: GoalCreateFields.title (NonBlankStr) -> GoalModel.title String(255) (db_models.py:1456); Goal.external_ref -> String(1000); ProjectCreate.name/description -> ProjectModel String(255)/String(1000) (db_models.py:157-158); ProjectGroupCreate.name -> String(255); VisualizationCreate.viz_type/file_path -> String(40)/String(1000) (db_models.py:1536-1537); AnalysisCreate.method_hash/code_version/environment_hash -> String(255); AcquisitionOutputCreate.file_path/checksum -> String(1000)/String(64). Services only strip and check non-empty (goal_service.py:120-125, project_service.py:78-79). By contrast dataset commit_hash IS validated (returns 422 for 300 chars), showing the intended pattern. Postgres enforces varchar(n) with SQLSTATE 22001 'value too long for type character varying(n)', which SQLAlchemy raises as DataError; routes/errors.py has no handler for it, so the generic Exception handler returns 500 'Internal server error.' SQLite ignores the declared length, so the same request succeeds there — the …

**Evidence.** Scratch test on the SQLite-backed client: POST /goals {title: 't'*600, external_ref: 'e'*1500} -> 201 with len(title)==600 stored; POST /projects {name:'n'*600, description:'d'*2000} -> 201; POST /datasets {commit_hash:'h'*300} -> 422 (only this one is bounded). db_models.py:1456 `title: Mapped[str] = mapped_column(String(255), nullable=False)`, :1536 `viz_type: Mapped[str] = mapped_column(String(40), nullable=False)`, :157-158 project name String(255)/description String(1000). routes/errors.py:166-179 maps any non-LabTrackerError to 500.

**Failure scenario.** On a dedicated/shared-provider instance running Postgres, a researcher pastes a 300-character goal title (or a 45-character viz_type such as 'peri-stimulus time histogram with confidence bands') -> INSERT fails with DataError -> client sees 500 'Internal server error.' with no field information, and the error log shows a stack trace instead of a validation issue. The identical request on a local SQLite instance …

**Suggested fix.** Add Field(max_length=...) constants shared between schemas and db_models (as already done for data stores via data_store_definition.py), or enforce the same limits in the services, so over-long values return 422 with a field-level issue on every backend.

**Verification.** verify=confirmed(medium)

#### M50. Analysis deletion check skips PROPOSED/TESTING claims and runs before the write, so a concurrent status promotion produces a SUPPORTED claim with no evidence

`src/lab_tracker/services/analysis_service.py:279` · concurrency · area: services-core-b

`_ensure_analysis_can_be_deleted` (analysis_service.py:367-387) is evaluated before `unit_of_work()` and ignores claims whose status is PROPOSED or REJECTED. `claim_analyses` rows cascade on analysis delete (db_models.py:1126-1138). `ClaimService.update_claim` promoting a PROPOSED claim to SUPPORTED validates `_ensure_claim_support_links` against the link rows it just read and then only updates the `claims` row; it takes no lock on the analysis. The managed deletion handler locks the `analyses` row FOR UPDATE, which protects against new link inserts (FK KEY SHARE) but not against a status UPDATE on `claims`. Interleaving delete(check sees PROPOSED -> allowed) with promote(read links OK -> UPDATE status) commits both, leaving a SUPPORTED claim with empty `supported_by_*`, which is exactly what `_ensure_claim_support_links` and the publication-readiness 'Supported claim has no dataset or analysis evidence' check exist to prevent. Dataset deletion avoids this by refusing while any claim references it (dataset_service.py:493-501) and locking; analysis deletion is the inconsistent path.

**Evidence.** analysis_service.py:375-377 `if claim.status in {ClaimStatus.PROPOSED, ClaimStatus.REJECTED}: continue`; :276-295 the check runs before `with self.unit_of_work()`; claim_service.py:298-302 support validation reads links, :338-339 saves only the claim; managed_deletions.py:246-252 locks `AnalysisModel` only.

**Failure scenario.** Under SQLite or Postgres READ COMMITTED: T1 DELETE /analyses/X passes the guard because the only claim citing X is PROPOSED; T2 PATCH /claims/C {status: supported} validates against the still-present link and commits; T1 commits and the cascade removes claim_analyses(C, X). Claim C is now SUPPORTED with no evidence, and readiness later flags the project as blocked with no actor having done anything invalid.

**Suggested fix.** Treat every claim link as blocking (as dataset deletion does), or perform the guard inside the write transaction after locking the referencing claim rows (SELECT ... FOR UPDATE on claims joined through claim_analyses) so a concurrent promotion serializes after the delete and fails validation.

**Verification.** verify=confirmed(medium)

#### M51. Claims can be deleted regardless of status or references; cascades strip evidence, logic edges and pivot invalidations

`src/lab_tracker/services/claim_service.py:349` · data-integrity · area: data-integrity

`delete_claim` only checks contributor access, then deletes. Datasets, analyses, questions and sessions all have `_ensure_*_can_be_deleted` guards, but a SUPPORTED/REJECTED claim with evidence, a claim that other claims `extends`/`refutes`/`depends_on` (claim_edges CASCADE on both claim_id and target_claim_id), a claim referenced by visualizations (visualization_claims CASCADE) or by a committed PIVOT exploration node (`invalidates_claim_id` SET NULL) is removed silently. The PIVOT node then violates its own 'invalidate exactly one node or claim' invariant and, being committed, can never be re-pointed (exploration_service.py:252 blocks content edits outside STAGED).

**Evidence.** claim_service.py:349-357; db_models.py ClaimEdgeModel FKs ondelete=CASCADE (lines ~1149-1160), ExplorationNodeModel.invalidates_claim_id ondelete=SET NULL (~1290). Scratch reproduction (test_supported_claim_can_be_deleted_and_strands_pivot_node): supported claim with dataset evidence + committed pivot node; `delete_claim` succeeds and the pivot reloads with `invalidates_claim_id=None, invalidates_node_id=None`.

**Failure scenario.** A contributor deletes a supported claim that a pivot node invalidates and that two other claims extend. The claim, its evidence links and all three claim edges vanish; the exploration trajectory now contains a committed pivot that invalidates nothing, and the PROV export loses the wasRevisionOf/logic edges.

**Suggested fix.** Add `_ensure_claim_can_be_deleted`: refuse when status != PROPOSED (route to REJECTED with a terminal_reason instead), when incoming/outgoing claim edges exist, or when exploration nodes reference it via target/evidence_refs/invalidates_claim_id; at minimum remove goal links and edges explicitly rather than by silent cascade.

**Verification.** verify=confirmed(medium)

#### M52. Claim-edge cycle check is read-then-write with no lock; concurrent edge creates commit a cycle

`src/lab_tracker/services/claim_service.py:376` · concurrency · area: data-integrity

`create_claim_edge` loads every edge in the project (`list_claim_edges`), runs `_ensure_claim_edge_would_not_cycle` in Python, then inserts. Unlike question-parent updates (which take `lock_project_question_dag`, a pg_advisory_xact_lock on Postgres) there is no lock at all: two concurrent requests adding A->B and B->A each see an acyclic graph and both inserts succeed (different (claim,target,relation) tuples, so `uq_claim_edges_claim_target_relation` does not help). The acyclicity invariant is therefore only enforced for sequential callers, on both Postgres and SQLite.

**Evidence.** claim_service.py:361-397 has no `lock_*` call; repository.py defines advisory locks for questions/datasets/experiments/sessions but nothing for claims. Reproduced on SQLite by interleaving the second request's edge read with the first request's commit (scratch test test_scratch_cycle_race.py::test_concurrent_claim_edges_commit_a_cycle_on_sqlite): printed `committed edge pairs: {(A,B),(B,A)}`, 'passed'. On Postgres the same interleaving needs no help: neither transaction locks any row the other touches.

**Failure scenario.** Two contributors (or one UI double-submit and an MCP agent) create depends_on edges A->B and B->A within the same window; both 201. `_ensure_claim_edge_would_not_cycle` and downstream claim-graph traversals now operate on a cyclic graph that the API promised could not exist.

**Suggested fix.** Serialize edge creation per project with the existing DAG lock (`self.repository.lock_project_question_dag(claim.project_id)` or a new claim-graph advisory lock domain) inside `application_transaction()`, and re-read edges after the lock, mirroring `update_question`.

**Verification.** verify=confirmed(medium)

#### M53. Inherited group-scoped stores are listed for a project member but GET /data-stores/{id} and /health return 404 for the same member; unscoped listing omits group stores entirely

`src/lab_tracker/services/data_store_service.py:186` · authz · area: store-authority

list_data_stores(project_id=P) authorizes with require_read(P) (project membership suffices) and returns list_effective_for_project(P), which includes stores scoped to P's group. get_data_store_for_read for a group-scoped store checks can_group_read(group_id), which requires an explicit group membership (group_membership_role only consults get_group_membership). A user who is a contributor on a project inside a group but not a group member therefore sees the inherited store in the project listing but receives 'Data store does not exist.' (404) from GET /data-stores/{id} and GET /data-stores/{id}/health. Separately, the unscoped GET /data-stores for a non-admin uses query(project_ids=accessible_project_ids) which filters on project_id only, so group-scoped stores never appear even for group members. The design doc (data-store-registry-design.md:414-419) says group stores are 'inherited by every project in the group'; the read/health authorization does not honour that inheritance. This is fail-closed, so not a data exposure, but it is an inconsistent authorization contract that makes …

**Evidence.** data_store_service.py:186-193: if store.project_id is not None: can_read = self.authorization.can_read(...); elif store.group_id is not None: can_read = self.authorization.can_group_read(store.group_id, actor=actor). :205-208 list path uses require_read + list_effective_for_project. :213-219 unscoped path uses query(project_ids=...). Reproduced with scratch test (scratchpad/p4/test_group_visibility_scratch.py): output 'project-scoped listing contains inherited group store: True', 'unscoped listing contains inherited group store: False', 'GET status: 404', 'health status: 404' for a contributor of a project in the group.

**Failure scenario.** Admin registers a group store 'lab-shared' for lab group G; scientist S is a contributor on project P in G but not added to G's member list. S lists P's stores and sees 'lab-shared', then clicks/fetches it or checks its health and gets 404 'Data store does not exist.'

**Suggested fix.** Decide one inheritance rule and apply it consistently: in get_data_store_for_read, treat a group-scoped store as readable when the actor can read the group OR can read any project in that group (mirroring list_effective_for_project), or alternatively filter inherited group stores out of the project listing when the actor lacks group read. Also include group-scoped stores for the actor's readable groups in the unscoped listing (or document that …

**Verification.** verify=confirmed(medium)

#### M54. Archived datasets that were committed are mutable: question links, manifest and commit hash can be rewritten

`src/lab_tracker/services/dataset_service.py:320` · data-integrity · area: services-core-a

The immutability guard in _update_dataset_in_transaction only fires when the *current* status is COMMITTED (`was_committed = current_status == DatasetStatus.COMMITTED`). Once a committed dataset is archived (the path the delete guard explicitly recommends: 'archive committed datasets'), its status is ARCHIVED, so a PATCH with question_links / commit_manifest / commit_hash passes the guard, rewrites the content-addressed manifest, recomputes commit_hash and even changes primary_question_id. The committed provenance record (which claims, analyses and PROV-O exports reference by commit_hash) is therefore not durable after archiving.

**Evidence.** dataset_service.py:319-323: was_committed = current_status == DatasetStatus.COMMITTED if was_committed and (is_provided(commit_hash) or is_provided(question_links) or is_provided(commit_manifest)): raise ValidationError("Committed datasets are immutable.") Scratch test (repository_backed_api): create COMMITTED dataset -> update_dataset(status=ARCHIVED, terminal_reason=...) -> update_dataset(commit_manifest=<new files>, question_links=[other primary]) succeeded: ARCHIVED MUTATED: status DatasetStatus.ARCHIVED hash changed True primary changed True files [DatasetFile(path='data.csv', checksum='zzz')] tests/test_core_api.py::test_committed_dataset_is_immutable only checks the COMMITTED state …

**Failure scenario.** Contributor commits dataset D (hash H) that a supported claim cites; later archives it with a reason; then PATCH /datasets/D with a new commit_manifest and question_links -> 200, D now has a different hash, different files and a different primary question while the claim and exports still describe H.

**Suggested fix.** Treat ARCHIVED as terminal-immutable for manifest/link/hash fields (e.g. `if current_status in {COMMITTED, ARCHIVED} and (...)`), or track a `was_ever_committed` flag; add a regression test that archives a committed dataset and asserts question_links/commit_manifest/commit_hash patches are rejected.

**Verification.** verify=confirmed(medium)

#### M55. Deleting datasets, sessions, notes, analyses, claims or visualizations leaves note targets (and staged-dataset source_session_id) dangling

`src/lab_tracker/services/dataset_service.py:479` · data-integrity · area: services-core-a

Only delete_question checks for notes that target the entity (_ensure_question_not_referenced). _ensure_dataset_can_be_deleted, delete_session/_ensure_session_can_be_deleted and _ensure_note_can_be_deleted do not, and note_targets.entity_id has no FK, so the deleted entity's id survives in every note that targeted it. Afterwards those notes are half-broken: any PATCH that includes targets fails with 404 'Dataset does not exist.' because validate_target re-validates all targets, and the project graph (_add_full_edges) emits edges to nodes that do not exist. delete_session likewise leaves staged datasets whose commit_manifest.source_session_id points at the deleted session; committing them later fails with 'Session does not exist.'.

**Evidence.** Scratch test: note targeting a staged dataset and an operational session; delete_dataset + delete_session succeed; then NOTE TARGETS AFTER DELETE: [EntityRef(DATASET, e7eb...), EntityRef(SESSION, 7ee3...)] api.update_note(note_id, targets=<same list>) -> NotFoundError db_models.py:564-574 NoteTargetModel.entity_id is a bare GUID with no ForeignKey. session_service.py:260-267 only blocks non-staged datasets.
