### HTTP route layer (`routes-http`)

The HTTP route layer is thin and consistent: every router pulls the actor from `request.state.auth_context` (set by the auth middleware, which allow-lists only a small set of public paths), resolves the request-scoped `LabTrackerAPI` or the typed `RequestHandlers` from request state, and delegates. Authorization is layered correctly for the surfaces I traced: detail GETs use `*_for_read` methods that return an opaque 404 for inaccessible targets, list routes go through `CatalogQueries._project_scope`, which either enforces `require_read` on an explicit project_id or passes `accessible_project_ids` down to repository queries that honour an empty set (and the parametrised scoping test plus the search/portfolio tests confirm it), and every mutation re-checks `require_contributor`/`require_owner` inside the service even where the route only did `ensure_project_read`. Cross-entity operations (claim edges, experiment membership, dataset files, visualization files) validate same-project ownership at the service/handler layer, downloads verify the file belongs to the addressed dataset, and `Content-Disposition` handling is robust (RFC 5987 fallback, path stripping). Uploads are bounded by both the Content-Length pre-check and the storage backends' `max_bytes` (wired from settings), and I verified that the Starlette middleware ordering guarantees the DB commit happens before background auto-transcription runs. The fragile spots are in the edges rather than the core: quick-capture stores bytes before authorization, the usage-event export is unbounded, `include` parsing on /search fails silently, authorization denials share the 401 status with authentication failures (a documented but semantically wrong choice), and idempotent replays report different status codes across endpoints. Downloads use manual Content-Length on StreamingResponse without Range support, which is acceptable for the retained surface but means large dataset files cannot be resumed. A few helpers are dead or duplicated, and the `api_from_request(request, api)` fallback argument is threaded through 179 call sites although it can never work, which slightly undermines the 'fail loud' story the handlers layer otherwise tells.

Strengths:
- Uniform read opacity: every detail GET goes through `get_*_for_read` and returns an identical 404 for inaccessible and missing targets; tests freeze this contract (test_core_read_opacity, test_project_graph_routes).
- List scoping is centralised in `CatalogQueries._project_scope` and the repository treats `project_ids=set()` as 'no rows', preventing the classic empty-filter-means-everything bug; `test_list_endpoint_scoping` covers eight list endpoints.
- Service layer re-enforces contributor/owner authorization on every mutation, so route-level `ensure_project_read` is defence in depth rather than the only gate.
- Cross-entity linking (claim edges, experiment sessions/datasets, dataset file downloads) validates same-project membership and file-to-dataset ownership before acting.
- Upload handling is bounded at two levels (Content-Length pre-check and `max_bytes` in both storage backends), content types are allow-listed, and dangerous types (HTML/SVG/JS) are blocked; attachment filenames are sanitised with an RFC 5987 fallback.
- Request-scoped transaction middleware commits only on <400 responses, defers storage cleanup to after-commit/after-rollback hooks, and the handler layer (`RequestHandlers`) keeps SQLAlchemy out of route modules, enforced by `test_route_application_boundary`.
- Query parameters that shape expensive work (graph search/neighbourhood limits, depth, max_nodes/max_edges, artifact byte bounds) are validated with explicit bounds at the route or schema level.

### App runtime and configuration (`runtime-config`)

The assembly layer is cleanly factored: `create_app()` composes an immutable `AppRuntime` (settings -> policies -> engine/session factory -> services -> admission/health probes) in a deliberate order that parses the sensitive store-authority registry first and fails before any side effect (logging, workdir, engine) on invalid policy, which tests enforce. Middleware order is correct (security headers -> auth -> admission -> per-request DB scope -> router), the request scope commits only on <400 responses and rolls back/closes exactly once on every path (including BaseException), and background workers use per-tick sessions with `unit_of_work` commits and cancellable `to_thread` loops. SQLite wiring is careful (foreign keys, WAL, busy_timeout, explicit legacy transaction control to avoid SQLITE_BUSY_SNAPSHOT), Postgres URLs are normalised to psycopg, and the backup path uses the online backup API over a read-only URI, verifies integrity, and coordinates with a flock sidecar plus a BEGIN EXCLUSIVE probe; I confirmed backups work on a WAL database after clean shutdown and from a read-only directory. Error handling is uniform (ErrorEnvelope, generic 500 body, JSON logs with provider-secret redaction) and validation-error responses do not echo input. The fragile parts are at the edges of the process: everything that keys off `request.client.host` (bootstrap-token disclosure, HSTS, rate-limit buckets) assumes trusted proxy headers that the shipped Dockerfile/compose never configure; `db.py` still carries an import-time `SessionLocal` engine that drags full Settings validation into every import (breaking the deployment probe's JSON contract); observability endpoints are authenticated but not role-gated and leak paths/DB error strings; and the BASE_URL fallback breaks under a root_path. Settings validation is extensive for resolver/admission knobs but inconsistent about secret hygiene (only one secret field is repr-excluded). Lifespan shutdown is orderly (cancel workers -> release lock -> dispose engine -> remove git workdir), though cancelled `to_thread` workers can still be mid-transaction when the engine is disposed and the lock acquisition is best-effort by design (a second server only logs a warning).

Strengths:
- Runtime composition parses the store-authority registry and all resolver/git/rclone policies before any side effect, with tests proving no logging, workdir, engine or client is created on invalid config and that secrets never appear in the error chain.
- Per-request DB scope (LabTrackerRequestScope) has clear commit/rollback/close semantics, deferred after-commit/after-rollback actions, idempotent close, and is covered by tests for success, error response, exception and BaseException-in-commit paths.
- SQLite connection configuration pins legacy transaction control and enforces foreign_keys/WAL/busy_timeout; alembic env reuses the same listener so migrations see identical invariants.
- Backup/restore uses sqlite3's online backup API via a percent-encoded read-only file: URI (handles '?', '#', Windows drive paths), verifies integrity read-only, and guards restore with both an advisory flock sidecar held for the server's lifetime and a BEGIN EXCLUSIVE probe; verified WAL backup …
- Error handlers map the exception hierarchy correctly (subclass handlers win), return a generic body for 500s, log with exc_info through a JSON formatter that redacts provider credentials, and never echo request input in 422 responses.
- Auth secret placeholders are rejected in non-local environments, auth cannot be disabled outside local, empty/whitespace secrets fail loud in TokenService/InvitationTokenService, and review-email enablement requires HTTPS BASE_URL and auth.
- Middleware ordering ensures admission controls run after authoritative authentication and before the ordinary request DB scope, matching the documented contract.
- Lifespan shutdown is nested try/finally so worker cancellation, lock release, engine disposal and workdir cleanup each run even if an earlier step fails (tested).

### Authorization sweep (`sec-authz`)

Authentication is a single Starlette middleware (app_parts/middleware.py) that classifies the bearer credential (JWT session, ldev_ device token, lpat_ personal access token), applies a coarse path/method policy for device and service principals, and stashes an AuthContext on request.state; everything else is enforced in the service layer through ProjectAuthorizationPolicy (direct membership, group-owner inheritance, group_read_all read-only inheritance, global admin bypass). I inventoried all 40 route modules (~190 endpoints) and the 29 read / 14 write MCP tools (which are pure HTTP proxies with no authorization of their own). Every entity read goes through a *_for_read helper that raises an opaque 404, every list/search/graph/export/provenance path scopes by accessible_project_ids or an explicit require_read, and every create/link path I traced (questions, datasets, sessions, notes, analyses, claims, claim edges, visualizations, experiments, exploration nodes, goals, data stores, collections, evidence bundles, graph drafts) checks that referenced sub-resources belong to the same project — I found no cross-project read or IDOR. Draft accept/commit additionally require an interactive principal, PAT scopes (all vs batch_run_due) are fail-closed, and run-due requires global admin. The fragile parts are at the credential and role boundary rather than the entity boundary: the PAT branch trusts the role frozen into the token row (a demoted admin's PAT stays admin, and nobody but the user can revoke it), the global viewer role is a ceiling for PATs but not for browser sessions (contradicting docs/configuration.md), and project-group detachment is unguarded, which undermines the group-owner oversight model that the retained surface relies on. Secondary hygiene issues are username oracles via membership-by-username and public registration, editor-writable supervision edges that feed provenance exports, and hosted templates that leave anonymous self-registration on. The layering is otherwise disciplined: routes only extract the actor and delegate, api_parts are thin usage-event wrappers, and the authorization policy is a single service that tests exercise directly.

Strengths:
- Consistent opaque-404 contract for unauthorized detail reads (get_*_for_read + OpaqueTargetNotFoundError), enforced by tests/test_core_read_opacity.py across projects, questions, notes, sessions, graph and export variants, including 'denied reads never touch storage / never record usage'.
- Every list/search/graph/portfolio/decision-context handler scopes rows through accessible_project_ids or an explicit require_read before querying (application/catalog_queries.py, context_queries.py); tests/test_list_endpoint_scoping.py covers the project-scoped list endpoints.
- Service-layer referential checks are thorough: primary/secondary questions, dataset ids, supporting analyses, answered questions, claim-edge targets, related claims, note targets, goal-link targets, exploration-node refs, experiment sessions/datasets, and source sessions are all validated to belong …
- Credential design is sound in most respects: SHA-256-hashed device/PAT/invitation secrets returned once, single-use atomic invitation and enrollment claims, HMAC-only JWT verification (no alg confusion; role claim ignored in favour of the DB), PAT TTL cap of 90 days, PAT role capped at the issuer's …
- Human-in-the-loop gates are enforced server-side: accept/commit of graph drafts and member onboarding require an interactive principal (USER/DEVICE), so PATs and the SYSTEM automation principal can propose but never commit; run-due requires global admin regardless of PAT scope.
- Bootstrap-admin token disclosure keys off the transport peer (request.client), never the Host header, with explicit never/first_run modes and the dedicated-instance template set to never.
- Group semantics match docs/retained-v1-surface.md exactly (owner inherits owner; viewer/contributor inherit nothing unless group_read_all, which yields read-only) and are pinned by tests/test_project_authorization_policy.py.
- Rate limiting and admission control (login/register, PAT verification failures, artifact resolution, store health) run after authoritative authentication so unauthenticated callers cannot consume per-actor capacity.

### Input-handling security sweep (`sec-input`)

The cross-cutting security posture of the backend is unusually strong for a lab tool. The layered design (routes -> handlers -> services -> repository) keeps input validation at the edge: every SQL `text()` is parameterised, LIKE patterns are escaped in one helper, stored files are addressed by server-generated UUIDs so filename input never touches a path, Content-Disposition headers are sanitised against CR/LF and non-ASCII, and all user-file downloads are `attachment` with nosniff plus a strict CSP on the app and file-serving prefixes. Outbound network access is exemplary: `outbound_http.py` resolves DNS once, validates every answer against a public/internal/forbidden scope (including IPv4-mapped, 6to4, Teredo and NAT64 rejection), pins the connection to approved sockaddrs, re-authorises each redirect, forbids TLS downgrade and enforces a single monotonic deadline; Git and rclone remotes are structurally parsed from operator allowlists, rebuilt rather than echoed, run without a shell under `GIT_ALLOW_PROTOCOL`/redirect-disabled config through a bounded, redacting subprocess executor. Secrets are handled carefully: provider errors and logs pass through `provider_error_message`, logs are JSON (no log injection), email composition is content-free with validated addresses and signed review links that are re-verified against the delivery row. The fragile parts are at the trust boundaries the code does not own: the first-admin bootstrap disclosure trusts the raw TCP peer, which every proxied/containerised deployment turns into a private address; the PWA service worker treats any POST navigation to the share-target path as an OS share and the capture page imports it without consent; and request-body bounding is enforced after Starlette has already spooled the whole multipart body, with an in-memory rate limiter that grows without bound. These are all deployment-topology or framework-ordering issues rather than logic bugs in the domain layer, and they concentrate in `app_parts/middleware.py`, `routes/auth.py`, `rate_limit.py` and `frontend/sw.js`.

Strengths:
- SSRF defence in outbound_http.py is thorough: DNS-pinned connections, mixed-scope/forbidden address rejection (IPv4-mapped, 6to4, Teredo, NAT64, link-local, CGNAT handling), conjunctive internal-exception configuration, redirect re-authorisation without HTTPS->HTTP downgrade, identity-only …
- Subprocess boundary (git_process.py, git_remote_policy.py, bounded_subprocess.py) never uses a shell, rebuilds remote spellings from parsed structure, rejects leading-hyphen hosts/segments/users, strips ambient GIT_* repo-selection variables, sets GIT_ALLOW_PROTOCOL and followRedirects=false, and …
- All raw SQL is parameterised (`pg_advisory_xact_lock(:lock_key)` etc.) and substring search escapes `\`, `%`, `_` centrally in sqlalchemy_repository_parts/common.py.
- File storage is UUID-sharded with atomic temp-file writes, client filenames are only stored as metadata, downloads are forced `attachment` with sanitised RFC 5987 filenames, and uploads are content-type allowlisted with HTML/SVG/JS explicitly blocked; nosniff/DENY/no-referrer headers apply globally …
- Review-link tokens are HMAC-signed with domain separation, canonical base64url, strict payload key set, expiry and re-verification against the loaded delivery row; the /r/ route redirects only to fixed same-origin paths and never leaks existence.
- Credential hygiene: JSON log formatter and graph-drafting errors run through provider_error_message (query/header/API-key/lab-tracker-bearer redaction); SMTP failures are mapped to fixed safe messages; DataStorePersistenceError hides internals; MCP inbound middleware strips the Authorization header …
- Bootstrap-token disclosure already resists the obvious Host-header spoof and has tests for loopback, remote and unknown peers; PAT verification is rate-limited and read-only PATs have an explicit, fail-closed scope policy.

### Core entity services (`services-core-a`)

The core entity services form a coherent layered design: routes/handlers resolve the actor and delegate to composed services (ProjectService, QuestionService, DatasetService, SessionService, NoteService, GoalService, ExperimentService, AcquisitionCollectionService) that share a ServiceContext/BaseService transaction abstraction and a single ProjectAuthorizationPolicy for membership and group-inherited roles. Authorization is applied consistently at service entry (require_contributor/require_owner before mutation, can_read with opaque 404s on reads, goal scope authorization across every linked project), and the idempotent-create pattern (client_capture_id lookup, savepoint-isolated insert, IntegrityError recovery, field-conflict 409) is implemented uniformly for projects, questions, notes and collection captures. Concurrency discipline is good where it exists: question DAG updates, refactors, experiment membership and session lifecycle commands take advisory locks and deliberately re-read state after waiting, and owner-count invariants are lock-protected. The fragile part of the area is referential integrity across entities: guards are enforced per entity type in ad hoc lists rather than from a shared reference registry, so notes' targets, manifest note_ids, question supersession edges and child parent edges are not covered, and DB-level cascades (SET NULL / CASCADE) silently paper over the gaps while JSON-embedded references (source_session_id, note_ids) have no DB protection at all. Status/immutability rules are likewise uneven: datasets are immutable only while COMMITTED (not after archiving), notes have no transition table so PATCH can bypass the documented archive-with-reason contract, and refactors bypass entity version recording. Goal link cleanup is thorough but its resilience is brittle: a single dangling link crashes listings, and projectless goals can be destroyed by a single project deletion. The assigned tests exercise the happy paths and the documented guards well, but there is essentially no coverage for post-archive mutation, PATCH-driven note status changes, deletion of superseding questions, dangling references, or race conditions between unlocked promotions and locked deletes.

Strengths:
- Uniform idempotent create pattern (normalize capture id, pre-lookup, savepoint-isolated insert via recoverable_unit_of_work, IntegrityError recovery, ConflictError listing mismatched fields) across projects, questions, notes and collection captures, with IdempotentCreateResult letting routes return …
- ServiceContext.application_transaction gives direct/background commands one commit/rollback boundary with after-commit/after-rollback hooks, and RepositoryUnitOfWork defers to request middleware when present; raw note assets are cleaned up on rollback/reuse.
- Authorization is applied at service entry points, group inheritance (owner -> owner, group_read_all -> viewer) is encoded in one policy class, reads use opaque not-found errors, and goals require read/contributor/owner on every linked project.
- Owner-count invariants (last owner of project/group) are protected with row locks and re-reads; offboarding requires records to be exported before revoking membership.
- Question DAG cycle prevention is validated against a freshly loaded project graph under a project-scoped advisory lock, and refactor/child reparenting validates cycles against the post-change graph.
- Deletion guards for questions (notes, experiments, datasets, sessions, claims) and datasets (status, experiments, claims, analyses), plus chunked goal-link cleanup that is tested at the row level.
- Collection capture is content-addressed with request-hash idempotency, lock-first ordering, and DB constraints cross-checked by tests.

Not fully read: SQLAlchemy repository internals (lock_* implementations, query_goals target filtering, records_attributed_to_user) were only read where a claim depended on …; member_onboarding checkpoint logic inside note_service was read but its interplay with member_onboarding_service was not traced.; Race-condition claim on promote_operational_session_to_dataset was not reproduced on PostgreSQL (code-path analysis only).


### Analysis, claims, ownership and export services (`services-core-b`)

This area sits cleanly in the layered design: routes call `LabTrackerAPI` facades in `api_parts/*`, which delegate to `BaseService` subclasses that read through `self.repository` and write through `unit_of_work()`/`application_transaction()`, with request middleware owning the transaction on HTTP. Authorization is consistently expressed through `ProjectAuthorizationPolicy` (contributor for writes, opaque 404 via `*_for_read` for reads) and cross-project reference validation is thorough for claims, analyses, visualizations, exploration nodes and goals. Claim/analysis lifecycle rules (status transition tables, `_ensure_claim_support_links`, immutability of committed analyses, terminal reasons) are centralised in `services/shared.py` and are well tested; the Ara export closure, opaque-scope handling and usage-event accounting in `record_export_service.py` are carefully written and thoroughly exercised by `tests/test_record_exports.py`. The visualization asset path (`file_commands.py`, `managed_deletions.py`) is the strongest part: row locks, compare-and-set, after-commit blob cleanup and Postgres race tests. The fragile parts are referential integrity across the exploration graph and the people layer: exploration-node targets are FK-less and no delete guard or readiness check covers them, so a routine question delete makes the goal Ara export 404 while readiness says clean (the most serious finding); ownership reassignment is a hand-maintained table list that has fallen behind the schema; supervision edges are writable and enumerable by any editor with no relational or group scoping. Two narrower concurrency gaps exist where one path locks and its sibling does not (visualization metadata PATCH vs. upload; analysis delete vs. claim promotion). Publication readiness is limited to the entity types it was written for and silently passes projects with dangling exploration references or broken pivots. Overall the code is disciplined and readable, but several invariants are enforced only at write time and the cross-cutting delete/reassign paths have not kept pace with newer entities (exploration nodes, claim edges, experiments, provenance links).

Strengths:
- Consistent authorization pattern: contributor checks on every write, opaque `*_for_read` 404s for reads, and same-project validation for every cross-entity reference (claims, visualizations, exploration refs, goal links, note targets).
- Claim/analysis lifecycle invariants are centralised (`_CLAIM_STATUS_TRANSITIONS`, `_ensure_claim_support_links`, `_IMMUTABLE_ANALYSIS_STATUSES`, terminal-reason helpers) and covered by `tests/test_analysis_claims_visualizations.py` and `tests/test_claim_invariants.py`.
- Visualization asset handling is robust: row locks before durable writes, checksum/size compare-and-set, orphan-free candidate cleanup, after-commit deletion of replaced blobs, and dedicated Postgres race tests.
- Record export scope closure (`_close_artifact_scope`, `_drop_out_of_scope`) plus opaque-target semantics and usage-event accounting are carefully reasoned and extensively tested, including spanning goals and targets deleted after authorization.
- Provenance-link detection is deterministic, idempotent, human-gated (no public create endpoint) and only accepted links render in PROV-O; tests cover direction, star topology, idempotence and rejection.
- Data-store registration orders RBAC -> semantic validation -> operator grant -> SQLite writer reservation -> insert, translates DB failures into parameter-free errors, and tests assert the ordering and that secrets never leak into messages.
- Entity versioning dedups identical snapshots, records change-set commit stamps, and version endpoints reuse the entity read authorization.

### Store authority and health (`store-authority`)

This area implements a layered, deny-by-default authority model for registered data stores: a pure, immutable StoreAuthorityRegistry parsed once at startup (strict JSON, bounded depth/size, typed local/HTTP/rclone/Git boundaries, semantic SHA-256 fingerprints, overlap rejection), registration-time authorization in DataStoreService that persists grant id + fingerprint, and use-time revalidation (store_authority_use) that detaches a canonical binding from the ORM row, releases the DB scope, and re-authorizes against the startup snapshot before any host I/O. Health probes consume only a factory-sealed StoreProbeTarget, are wrapped in a bounded TTL/LRU cache with exact-key single-flight, and sit behind a separate no-wait admission middleware that runs before the DB session opens. Path confinement is split cleanly: LocalFilesystemAuthority does purely lexical, I/O-free grant selection (rejecting '..', '.', empty components, UNC/device namespaces, control characters, and provably-escaping suffixes) and hands one root plus the raw candidate to an isolated `python -I -S -B` helper that resolves components with retained O_NOFOLLOW directory descriptors, re-checks absolute link targets against the root spellings, enforces a nested store-root boundary for registered reads, and verifies regular-file stability before/after streaming. Git and rclone remotes are parsed structurally into canonical values (no option-shaped components, no embedded credentials, IDNA-strict hosts, prefix-by-segment matching), argv is composed only from approved typed values behind `--`, environments are sanitized, and Git pins require full nonzero SHA-1/SHA-256 IDs fetched by object id. The design is careful and well tested (~15k lines of tests), and I found no injection, traversal, or cross-tenant defect in the assigned modules. The fragility is in integration and contract consistency rather than the primitives: local_fs health is silently unreachable behind a static 'temporarily unavailable' result while the retained surface documents it as shipped (and registered local reads are likewise closed pending a bead), group-store inheritance is honoured by listing but not by read/health authorization, and a legacy realpath-based LocalPathPolicy survives as dead code with a divergent parser on the …

Strengths:
- Registry parsing is genuinely pure and bounded (char/byte/depth/node/key/list caps, duplicate-key rejection, no NaN/Infinity, no raw values retained in errors), and authorization is explicit-ID only with scope, kind, structural containment, credential mode and capability subset all required.
- Use-time revalidation detaches an exact canonical binding from the ORM row and checks both returned grant_id and fingerprint, so same-ID grant expansion is detected; the registry snapshot is fixed at startup so cache entries cannot outlive a revocation across restart.
- Local path confinement never canonicalizes or stats a candidate in-process; the helper walks with retained no-follow descriptors, bounds symlink hops/steps, requires absolute link targets to re-enter the same root spelling, and adds the registered store root as a nested boundary for registered …
- Regular-file reads verify S_ISREG on the opened descriptor, snapshot size/mtime/ctime/inode before and after streaming, and treat any EOF-proof byte as a terminal failure, so FIFO/device/replacement races fail closed.
- Git and rclone policies reject option-shaped components, backslashes, percent escapes, '=', '?', '#', local/drive/helper forms and embedded credentials; argv is composed only from canonical typed values, Git uses '--' plus a ls-remote --get-url preflight to defeat insteadOf rewrites, …
- Git pins accept only full lowercase nonzero SHA-1/SHA-256 object IDs, fetched by object id into a per-format cache and read via cat-file after a size check, so resolved content is content-addressed and immutable.
- The health cache is hard-bounded LRU with TTL starting at probe completion, exact-key single-flight with bounded follower waits, and never caches exceptions; followers get only a sanitized static exception.
- Admission middleware for health runs before the DB session middleware, matches the root-path-relative route exactly, and always releases its lease, so saturation cannot hold a database connection.

### Bounded subprocess and platform helpers (`subprocess-platform`)

This area is the process boundary of the application: BoundedSubprocessExecutor (bounded_subprocess.py) runs every external binary (git, rclone, the two local-filesystem helpers) under one absolute monotonic deadline with independent stdout/stderr byte caps, POSIX process-group or Windows Job Object containment, stdin=DEVNULL, close_fds=True and fully redacted errors; git_process.py and local_filesystem_operations.py sit on top as thin, typed seams, and the helper modules are stand-alone scripts run with `python -I -S -B` that receive a canonical JSON request via one environment variable and answer with a fixed exit status plus opaque bytes. The design is strong where it matters most: the pre-follow traversal on POSIX (lstat before openat, O_NOFOLLOW/O_PATH/O_DIRECTORY, absolute links re-anchored to a retained root descriptor, `..` bounded by a boundary depth, dev/ino re-verification after open, identity-based cycle detection) and on Windows (NtCreateFile relative to retained handles with OBJ_DONT_REPARSE/FILE_OPEN_REPARSE_POINT, reparse buffers parsed by hand, strict FILE_ID_128 identity checks) is careful and well tested for escapes, loops, races on replaced components, output-free failure and reverse-order handle cleanup; the Windows suspended-spawn/assign/verify/resume ordering in _windows_job.py is testable without kernel32 and closes every handle exactly once. HTTP entry points invoke the resolver from sync `def` routes, so the blocking subprocess work stays off the event loop. The fragile parts are the failure policy and the containment assumptions rather than the security boundary: both enumeration helpers abort the entire recovery scan on static, non-race conditions (dangling symlinks, unreadable subdirectories, NTFS-legal-but-Win32-illegal names), which in practice disables the documented content-hash recovery feature on many real lab trees; POSIX containment is process-group based, so a setsid() descendant escapes it, and the non-daemon reader threads then block interpreter shutdown; and on the ordinary success path the leader is reaped by poll() before the group is signalled, contrary to the module's own comment. On the already-known PID-1 reaping issue I add one detail: in this sandbox (PID 1 = process_api, no reaping) the test that fails is the …

Strengths:
- Executor never exposes argv, cwd, env or raw stderr: all failures are mapped to fixed-message ProcessExecutionError subclasses, including exceptions raised by Popen audit hooks (Exception, KeyboardInterrupt and other BaseException are each handled explicitly).
- Pipe drains never allocate more than limit-count+1 bytes per read, stdout and stderr have independent caps, and a zero limit correctly rejects the first byte while allowing empty output (verified by tests/test_bounded_subprocess.py).
- Cleanup has its own fixed, validated bound (terminate grace + kill grace, both <= 5 s) separate from the execution deadline; retries stop at the bound; EINTR/ESRCH/EPERM/EIO are classified and Darwin's EPERM-for-zombie-group case is handled explicitly.
- POSIX helper resolves every candidate component relative to retained directory descriptors (lstat then openat with O_NOFOLLOW, dev/ino re-check after open, searchability probe via faccessat AT_EACCESS), bounds symlink expansions, steps, components and path bytes, and re-anchors absolute links only …
- Windows helper does the same with NtCreateFile relative to retained handles, hand-parsed REPARSE_DATA_BUFFERs limited to mount-point/symlink tags with DOS-drive targets, FILE_ID_128 identities for cycle and race detection, and the registered-store boundary is non-popable; the fake ABI tests check …
- Helper protocol is strict: request must be canonical sorted ASCII JSON <= 24 KiB with an exact key set, responses are canonical JSON <= 8 MiB re-validated by the parent (duplicate candidates, ordering of preferred vs fallback, root_index bounds, locator budget), and file reads prove EOF with one …
- Windows Job Object lifecycle is created non-inheritable with KILL_ON_CLOSE, the child is spawned CREATE_SUSPENDED and resumed only after IsProcessInJob confirms membership, and close_containment is the tree-wide backstop when TerminateJobObject fails.
- git_process strips repository-selection variables, forces GIT_TERMINAL_PROMPT=0, pins GIT_ALLOW_PROTOCOL and GIT_CEILING_DIRECTORIES, and disables HTTP redirects both generically and for the exact approved URL.

### Backend test suite quality (`tests-quality`)

The backend test suite (208 files, ~2,840 test functions expanding to 4,900+ parametrized cases, 89% line coverage of lab_tracker) is unusually disciplined for a research tool: it favours hand-written fakes (tests/http_security_fakes.py, tests/store_authority_fakes.py) over unittest.mock (essentially zero MagicMock use; ~457 monkeypatch.setattr calls, concentrated in filesystem/CLI modules), registers every engine/session for deterministic teardown with ResourceWarning promoted to an error, uses a FakeClock for deadlines instead of wall-clock sleeps, and runs SQLite-backed HTTP tests against an Alembic-migrated per-test database via the conftest `client` fixture. Authorization is tested at both the policy layer (test_project_authorization_policy.py) and the HTTP layer (list scoping, read-opacity suites certified against an OpenAPI-checked inventory, PAT/device/service-principal scope tests with explicit fail-closed negative cases), and atomicity tests exercise the real application transaction rather than mocks. The fragile parts are at the edges of the layered design: (1) a large share of unit tests, including all of test_auth_routes.py, build their schema with Base.metadata.create_all rather than the Alembic head, and nothing asserts the two agree — an autogenerate comparison shows 32 differences today (a missing FK, NOT NULL constraints the ORM declares but production DBs do not have, and indexes only migrations create); (2) three Postgres race tests are missing the `postgres` marker and are therefore skipped in the default job and deselected by `-m postgres` in the Postgres job, so they never run in CI; (3) two SQLite transaction-mode tests only run on Python 3.12+ while CI pins 3.10/3.11; (4) coverage is collected with `--cov` but never reported, enforced or uploaded. Tests are reasonably distributed across modules (largest files are artifact_resolution and the Windows/POSIX store-health helpers, which are also the largest source modules); the lowest-covered production modules are ownership.py (63%), deployment_probe.py, review_email_service.py and auth.py (82%, mostly the in-memory no-session-factory branches and one dead method). A few tests create an app with the default `sqlite:///./lab_tracker.db` URL and so touch whatever database sits in the …

Strengths:
- Engine/session lifecycle is enforced: api_helpers.register_test_resources + the autouse _drain_test_db_resources fixture, and pyproject filterwarnings promotes ResourceWarning to an error, so leaked SQLite connections fail the owning test.
- Fakes over mocks: http_security_fakes.py (FakeClock, FakeAddressResolver, RecordingSocket, RecordingSslContext) and store_authority_fakes.py mint real registry proofs so a test cannot assert against a binding production would reject; unittest.mock is essentially unused.
- Authorization has real negative tests at policy level (viewer/contributor/owner/group inheritance/last-owner guards) and HTTP level (parametrized list scoping over 8 endpoints, 50-variant read-opacity inventory certified against the OpenAPI operationIds, PAT scopes and device tokens with explicit …
- Atomicity and race tests exercise real transactions: nested application_transaction commits exactly once, commit failures reset hooks, savepoint replay preserves outer writes, invitation acceptance/revocation races use a Barrier with two threads, and Postgres advisory-lock races exist for datasets, …
- Migration guards: single-head test with an explanatory message, empty-to-head chain test, head/base/head round-trip, backfill guard tests, a migrated-database API round trip, wheel-installed migration smoke test, and packaging tests that assert package-data patterns cover every migration and …
- No reliance on wall-clock sleeps in assertions: the only time.sleep calls are inside child processes for bounded-subprocess tests or tiny writes in artifact_resolution; deadline behaviour uses FakeClock.
- Architecture guard tests (test_route_application_boundary.py, tests/typing/contracts.py under mypy) keep routes from importing SQLAlchemy or reaching raw session state.

## Completeness critic

A final agent was asked what the review missed, given every confirmed finding and each reviewer's declared coverage gaps. Its report follows verbatim; concrete defects it found were not put through the verification pipeline.

## Concrete defects found in this pass

- **src/lab_tracker/services/exploration_service.py:352 — medium (data integrity).** `delete_exploration_node` has no referrer guard and no lock. Reproduced (scratch test, FK pragma ON via project conftest): commit a PIVOT that invalidates node D and lists D as parent, delete D → PIVOT is left `status=COMMITTED, invalidates_node_id=None, invalidates_claim_id=None, parent_node_ids=[]` (FK `SET NULL` + edge `CASCADE`), violating the "exactly one invalidation target" rule that `_ensure_invalidated_refs` enforces on every write. Also reproduced: `question_service.delete_question` (:745) never consults `exploration_nodes.target_entity_*` (no FK), so the node's target dangles afterwards. No test in the repo exercises `delete_exploration_node` at all. This contradicts refuted item "Committed PIVOT nodes silently lose their invalidation target" — at least the node-deletion path is real.
- **src/lab_tracker/services/supervision_service.py:29,92,126 — medium (authorization/integrity).** Supervision edges are gated only by `require_role(actor, WRITE_ROLES)` (`{ADMIN, EDITOR}`), so any global editor can create, rewrite or delete supervision edges between *any* two users (including naming themselves supervisor of an admin) and list every edge instance-wide. Edges feed `actedOnBehalfOf` in every provenance document and record export (`record_export_service.py:95`). `tests/test_supervision_routes.py` only asserts admin-allowed and viewer-denied; the editor case is untested and undocumented. Edges do not confer access, so not critical.
- **src/lab_tracker/services/graph_draft_context.py:251 — low (perf).** `_source_notes_for_capture` calls `list_notes(project_id=…)` (`limit=None`, note_service.py:731) and filters `metadata.capture_bundle_id` in Python for every note-scoped draft; same class as **L104**/**L101**.
- **src/lab_tracker/services/record_export_service.py:398,604,701 — low (hygiene).** `if project_ids:` treats an empty scope set as "no filter" (repository.py:660 correctly uses `is not None and not project_ids`). Unreachable today because `require_goal_read` never returns an empty set for a linked goal, but it is the one place the "empty set = nothing" contract is inverted.
- **src/lab_tracker/services/experiment_service.py:225-280, collection_service.py:74 — low.** Write paths call `get_*` before `require_contributor`, giving non-members a 401-vs-404 existence oracle on experiments/sessions; same class as confirmed **refuted:graph-drafting-6** but the refuted **refuted:evidence-provenance-3** says the opposite — needs one ruling.

Spot-checked with no defect found: `application/catalog_queries.py` scope helper, `publication_readiness_service.py`, `collection_service.capture_snapshot` idempotency, `portfolio_query.py` and `graph_query.py` (420-1060) project scoping, `lab_tracker_client/outbox.py`, `deployments/dedicated-instance/{backup,common}.sh`, PAT/device routes (TTL bounded, consume rate-limited), entity-version routes, `useLocalDraft.js`, `routing.jsx`, `deploy/mcp/Caddyfile`, and the empty-`project_ids` guard across all repository parts (no truthiness bug).

## Uncovered or under-read areas worth a follow-up

- `services/exploration_service.py`, `services/experiment_service.py` — no services area owned them — delete/lock/oracle class confirmed above; `_ensure_dag` (:427) is read-then-write without a lock like **M52**.
- `services/supervision_service.py` role model — only touched for a TypeError — see defect above; also `query_supervision_edges(limit=None)` is global on every export/provenance read (**L40** confirmed, yet **L40** refuted the same fact).
- `services/graph_draft_context.py`, `graph_draft_review.py`, `graph_draft_validation.py`, `graph_draft_applier.py` (~3.1k lines) — nobody audited *what leaves the server* in provider context packets (image bytes, bundle-mate notes, other users' notes) or provider payload size; sec-input admitted skipping it.
- `mcp_evidence_bundle.py:254` — reads arbitrary host paths for snapshot uploads; verify it is subject to the same hosted-mode concern as **M35**/**M35**.
- `_windows_job.py` (635 lines, never read) and the unsampled 3/4 of `_windows_local_store_health_helper.py` — handle/cleanup class of **L48**/**L82**, only executable on Windows CI.
- `alembic/versions/0013_retained_v1_cleanup.py` — drops three tables; downgrade recreates columns only, not tables (irreversible downgrade, undocumented).
- Frontend `app-shell.jsx` (661) and `shared/ui.jsx` (405) — not reviewed by any frontend area though every panel depends on them.
- `services/publication_readiness_service.py:56-70` — eight `limit=None` project loads per request; perf class of **M48**.
- The June 2026 review's 71 MEDIUM findings were not re-checked; several of this pass's medium delete/dangling findings look like their descendants.

## Cross-cutting patterns

- **No referrer inventory.** **M51**, **M63**, **M68**, **M67**, **M55**, **L100**, **L110** and the exploration defect share one root: delete guards are hand-written per service while the schema has ~60 CASCADE/SET NULL FKs plus FK-less JSON references (note targets, `evidence_refs`, exploration targets, manifest note_ids). A generic "who references X" query plus a parity test (the same shape as the missing ORM/migration parity test **M23**/**M81**/**M81**) would close the class.
- **Global-role checks are the weak layer.** **L106**, **L105**, **L129** and the supervision defect: `require_role(WRITE_ROLES)` guards instance-wide entities with no per-user ownership, and tests only cover admin/viewer extremes.
- **Python-side filtering after `limit=None` loads** (**M46**, **M48**, **L40**, **L86**, **L104**, **L118**, **L101**, **L97**, plus context/readiness above) traces to the repository lacking JSON-metadata and relation filters.
- **Inconsistent adjudication of identical facts**: 401-vs-404 oracle confirmed (**refuted:graph-drafting-6**) and refuted (**refuted:evidence-provenance-3**); global supervision load confirmed (**L40**) and refuted (**L40**); pivot-invalidation loss refuted (**refuted:services-core-b-7**) but reproducible. The synthesis should re-adjudicate these three.
- **Triplicate reporting**: **M43**/**M43**/**M43** (rate limiter), **M83**/**M83** and **M5**/**M5** (uv.lock / `uv run`), **M45**/**M45** (401 vs 403), **L41**/**L41**, **L12**/**L12** are the same findings across sweeps.

## Severity calibration notes

- **H7** (MCP path traversal via IDs) + **M36** (open-ADMIN guard fails open): together a remote agent reaches `/auth/users` with an admin LPAT — jointly critical, not high+medium.
- **M25** (cross-origin POST silently imports content into the victim's project) is content injection against an authenticated user; high fits better than medium beside **M18**/**M18**.
- **M35** (medium) and **M35** (low) are the same hosted file-read/oracle issue; pick one level.
- **M79** (1 s git timeout records a dirty tree as clean in provenance) undermines the product's core provenance claim; medium reads low relative to **H9**/**H10** at high.
- **H8** (medium) is the root cause of **H8** (high); either merge or raise **H8**.
- **C1** critical is justified only because SQLite is a supported production backend; the note should say so explicitly.


### Lead rulings on the critic's points

- The exploration-node deletion defect is added to the findings as **M57** (single critic pass, reproduced with a scratch test). The earlier refutation of the pivot-invalidation claim stands for the path it examined; node deletion is a different path.
- Editor-wide authority over supervision edges is ruled a confirmed medium (**M71**); see the ruling recorded under that finding.
- The mutation-side 401-versus-404 existence difference is documented policy (`docs/read-opacity-inventory.md` excludes mutations and a test pins it), so the draft-mutation and provenance-link items are both refuted, and the experiment/collection instances the critic lists fall under the same ruling. If the policy changes, all of them change together.
- The whole-table supervision-edge load on provenance reads is ruled a confirmed low (**L39**).
- Duplicate pairs the critic listed are folded in this version of the report.
- Calibration: the MCP path-traversal and fail-open admin-guard pair (**H7**, **M36**) are kept at their individual levels but are listed together under the first priority for the hosted surface, because combined they let a remote agent reach any route the held token's scope allows. The cross-origin share-target import (**M25**) stays at the verifier's medium; the critic's argument for high (content injection into a research record by an unauthenticated third party) is noted and is a fair reading. The 1-second git timeout (**M79**) stays medium.

Generated from the review session's verified findings. Verification lenses per finding: `verify` (reproduce, prior art, reachability, calibration), `refute` (adversarial tie-breaker), `impact-single` (single calibration pass for lows), `lead` (first-hand check by the lead session). Bracketed suffixes on a lens name identify the duplicate finding that vote came from.
